While all precautions have been taken to \nensure the accuracy of information, neither the editor, nor New Media and CCID, can be held liable for any inaccuracies, injury or damages that may arise. \n\n5 17 27\n\n45 53 59\n\nhis guide to just some of the many things our downtown precinct has to offer, is brought to you by the Cape Town Central City Improvement District (CCID). We’re a non-profit company that operates within a very specific geographic area (see the map of our boundaries on pg 62) and it is our job to ensure that our CBD is SAFE | CLEAN | CARING | OPEN FOR BUSINESS. We are here to ensure that your experience of the Central City is the best it can be, and to do this we provide top-up services to those of our primary partners at the City of Cape Town and the South African Police Service (SAPS). We operate across four departments, namely, Safety & Security, Urban Management, Social Development and Communications. You’ll see our CCID presence clearly throughout the CBD, from the branded black and green uniforms of our 300 public safety officers, our teams of professional cleaners in yellow overalls and the members of our Straatwerk work opportunity programmes involved in everything from graffiti removal to road and pavement maintenance. For more information on the work we do, please visit our website capetownccid.org where you will also find hundreds more venues to explore. Or catch us on Facebook (facebook.com/CapeTownCCID and facebook. com/CityViewsCapeTown) as well as on Twitter (@CapeTownCCID) and Instagram (instragram.com/CapeTownCCID).\n\nCONTACT US 021 286 0830; email: info@capetownccid.org;\n\nvisit WWW.CAPETOWNCCID.ORG\n\nFacebook.com/CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\nWELCOME TO THE\n\nCAPE TOWN\n\nCENTRAL CITY\n\nOur CCID Public Safety Officers are on duty 24/7 throughout our area and you can always get hold of us anywhere within the Cape Town CBD by calling the CCID 24-hour CONTROL CENTRE NUMBER: 082 415 7127\n\nSAFETY & SECURITY\n\nThe CCID’s Safety & Security department deploys 300 Public Safety Officers 24/7 from our Control Centre, which works closely with the SAPS and the City’s Law Enforcement agencies. The Control Centre is available at all times via the CCID hotline number (082 415 7127). We provide complementary services towards a visible security presence:\n\n• Reaction units \n• Monitoring of traffic infringements \n• ATM fraud detection \n• Regulation of informal trading \n• Public assistance \n\nURBAN MANAGEMENT\n\nMonitoring the spaces “inbetween” the buildings of the CBD, from the roads and sidewalks to public squares, falls under the ambit of the CCID Urban Management department. There are 60 dedicated cleaners from a professional service provider working around the clock to keep the Central City neat and tidy. In turn, a 300-strong force of semi skilled cleaners and a skilled road maintenance team also operate in the CBD via a job creation programme conducted in partnership with local NGO Straatwerk.\n\nSOCIAL DEVELOPMENT\n\nThe CCID’s Social Development team – headed up by a registered social worker supported by two registered auxiliary social workers and one experienced field worker – monitor the homeless and indigent people who live on the street of the CBD. Working closely with six partner NGOs that service the area and provide for both adults and children, the team also promotes public awareness through the CCID’s “Show You Care” campaign. For more info on our NGO partners, visit www.showyoucare. co.za\n\nTHE CCID PROVIDES THE FOLLOWING TOP-UP SERVICES\n\nCOMMUNICATIONS\n\nThe CCID’s Communications department supports the three operational divisions of the organisation, promoting the work we do, as well as promoting investment in the Central City. To this end it produces a wide range of publications annually, and runs a number of public-awareness campaigns. It communicates across all platforms, from hard copy to online and including social media.\n\nEATERIES IN 100 OUR GUIDE +\n\nBEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY\n\nSIGHTS AND SITES IN OUR GUIDE50\n\n+\n\nFOR MORE PLACES, VISIT WWW.CAPETOWNCCID.ORG\n\n##### 6 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020 CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\nMUSEUMS\n\nIziko Museums This is a fascinating amalgamation of 14 national museums, 11 of which are located in and around the Central Business District. Collections cover natural history, social history and art, jewellery and artefacts providing insights into SA’s cultural heritage. The Planetarium, which was recently upgraded, offers an unmissable experience. iziko.org.za\n\nIziko Planetarium P 9\n\n25 Queen Victoria Street, 021 481 3900 An upgrade has rendered the star machine old school and obsolete. These days students can see the Milky Way – and beyond – by way of a full-dome theatre. The new technology allows scientists to visualise data from the Square Kilometre Array (SKA), Southern African Large Telescope (SALT) and MeerKAT radio telescope. Shows for teenagers as well as adults take place daily, while it falls to Davy Dragon to entertain five- to 10-year-old astronomers on the weekends. iziko.org.za\n\nMuseum of Gems & Jewellery\n\nP 3 Cnr Loop and Hout streets, 021 422 0148 Run by Prins & Prins jewellers, this museum tells the story of when diamonds began forming three billion years ago. Exhibits include notable South African finds, such as the Cullinan Diamond, the world’s largest gem-quality rough diamond. prinsandprins.com\n\nSouth African Jewish Museum\n\nP 9 88 Hatfield Street, 021 465 1546 South Africa’s Jewish community includes renowned figures such as novelist Nadine Gordimer and painter Irma Stern. The Isaac Kaplan netsuke (Japanese miniatures) collection is one of the world’s largest privately held collections. Africa’s first Holocaust Centre mixes interpretive panels and archival photos with recreated environments and multimedia displays. Only people aged 16 years and over are allowed. sajewishmuseum.co.za, holocaust.org.za\n\nDistrict Six Museum P 26\n\n25A Buitenkant Street, 021 466 7200 During the 1970s, residents of the area between the Central City and Woodstock, known as District Six, were forcibly removed and their homes flattened under the Group Areas Act, an apartheid law that forbade interracial neighbourhoods. The museum takes you on an interactive trip of the past. districtsix.co.za\n\n##### District Six Museum visit Visit capetownccid.org\n\nCLOSEST PARKING (SEE PAGE 63)\n\nWALLET FRIENDLY\n\nA TREAT\n\nSPECIAL OCCASION\n\nCHILD FRIENDLY\n\nWHEELCHAIR FRIENDLY P\n\nPLACES OF WORSHIP\n\nAuwal Masjid P 10\n\n39 Dorp Street, 082 551 7324 South Africa’s oldest mosque was established in 1794. It is recognised as a symbol of the Cape Muslims’ struggle for the recognition of Islam and their right to practise the religion. auwalmasjid.co.za\n\nCentral Methodist Mission\n\nP 10 Cnr Longmarket and Burg streets, 021 422 2744 This church opened in 1879. Its Gothic Revival interior features stained-glass windows, a large organ filling an arch and marble plaques remembering missionaries, soldiers and colonial characters. cmm.org.za\n\nDie Groote Kerk P 10\n\nAdderley Street, 021 422 0569 \nThis venerable old church was the first \nChristian place of worship erected in the \nCape. Its cornerstone was laid in 1700 \nand it remains the mothership of the Dutch \nReformed Church in Cape Town. It houses \nSouth Africa’s biggest organ, which has \n5 917 pipes. \ngrootekerk.org.za \n\nEvangelical Lutheran Church\n\nP 3 98 Strand Street, \n021 421 5854 \nDating back to 1774, this is South Africa’s \noldest church in permanent service and \nforms part of the country’s oldest city block. \nIt is historically a German church, but offers \nservices in English and Afrikaans too. Look \nout for the carvings inside by German sculptor \nAnton Anreith, whose work is regarded as the \npinnacle of the Cape Baroque style. \nlutheranchurch.org.za \n\nQ What do you love most about the city vibe?\n\nA There is a vibrancy and cosmopolitan feel in the city, unlike the outlying suburbs. It’s also a city with many dimensions; heritage buildings interspersed with ultra-modern high-rises. The City Bowl comprises a small area between the mountain and the sea, so everything’s close; you are never too far from some of the world’s major attractions.\n\nQ What is your favourite restaurant in the CBD?\n\nA There are so many! But if I have to choose, I’d say Fyn. I was lucky enough to have lunch there recently and apart from the beautiful food and décor, the service was outstanding.\n\nQ What is your favourite place to do some shopping in the CBD?\n\nA I am a lover of vintage, and always on the lookout for something unique. Church Street and Church Square are favourites. You’ll definitely find a few gems there – such as Babette’s Clothing. It’s also home to some of our most renowned art galleries (my favourite being Smith Studio) and on certain days of the week there’s an antique market with more vintage offerings.\n\nQ If you are in the city for a couple of days what is the one thing you need to experience?\n\nA The Company’s Garden, without a doubt. It’s a little piece of paradise and home to the oldest garden in the city, the Iziko South African National Gallery, the Cape Town Holocaust and Genocide Centre and the Planetarium. There is also a restaurant among the old trees where tame squirrels and pigeons scamper around for any leftovers.\n\nPEOPLE OF\n\nTHE CITY\n\nDebbie Loots, magazine editor and award-winning novelist of Split and Die Boek van Gelukkige Eindes, shares her perspectives on life in the city.\n\n##### 8 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020 CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\nGreat Synagogue P 9\n\n88 Hatfield Street, 021 465 1405 The Great Synagogue is also known as the Gardens Shul. Considered to be South Africa’s mother synagogue, this beautiful building features a number of distinctive domes and towers. The neighbouring Old Synagogue (1863) is South Africa’s oldest. gardensshul.org\n\nPalm Tree Mosque P 10\n\n185 Long Street, 082 551 7324 Also known as the Church of Jan van Bougies, after the owner of the property in 1811, the second oldest mosque in the country was named for the two palm trees in front of it – one remains. The building was once a house; a prayer room was opened later and it gained mosque status in 1825. muslim.co.za\n\nSt George’s Cathedral P 10\n\n5 Wale Street, 021 424 7360 \nThis grand Herbert Baker structure was built \nin 1906, but a cathedral has stood in this \nspot since 1847, which is why it’s considered \nsouthern Africa’s oldest cathedral. Archbishop \nEmeritus Desmond Tutu led the Cape Town \nPeace March, a pivotal anti-apartheid protest, \nfrom this Anglican cathedral in September \n1989. \nsgcathedral.co.za \n\nSt Mary’s Cathedral P 25\n\n16 Roeland Street, 021 461 1167 Also known as the Cathedral Church of Our Lady of the Flight into Egypt, this Catholic church was consecrated in 1851. It is the seat of the Metropolitan Archbishop of Cape Town. stmaryscathedral.org.za\n\nThe Unitarian Church P 11\n\n64 Hout Street \nNow 150 years old, this church welcomes \neveryone, regardless of beliefs, colour, sexual \norientation or ethnicity. \ncapetownunitarians.org \n\nTOUR COMPANIES\n\nAfrican Touch Tours\n\n072 388 2398 \nAfrican Touch offers a range of private guided \ntours, including a three-hour amble around \nthe Central City. Starting at St Georges Mall, \nthe tour takes in sights including The \nCompany’s Garden, the Slave Lodge, \nGreenmarket Square, Grand Parade, City \nHall, District Six Museum and the Castle of \nGood Hope. A perfect, quick and easy way to \nget an oveview of the city. \nafricantouchtours.com \n\nCity Sightseeing Cape Town P 2\n\n81 Long Street, 021 511 6000 With audio guides to give you more information about the area, City Sightseeing’s red double-decker buses are a fun way to get your bearings. The hop-on, hop-off service follows various loops around the CBD and peninsula. There are various different routes to choose from. The company also offers a boat service from the CTICC. citysightseeing.co.za\n\nDetour Africa P 5\n\n303A Long Street, 021 424 1115 With more than 15 years experience in the travel business, Detour Africa offers a\n\nCity Sightseeing Cape Town\n\nvisit Visit capetownccid.org\n\nCLOSEST PARKING (SEE PAGE 63)\n\nWALLET FRIENDLY\n\nA TREAT\n\nSPECIAL OCCASION\n\nCHILD FRIENDLY\n\nWHEELCHAIR FRIENDLY P\n\nCapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID carefully selected range of overland and travel packages that include everything from day trips to longer excursions. Whether you’re in the market for a day tour, an overnight camp or an exclusive lodge, they will help you devise a tour that suits your budget. detourafrica.co.za\n\nWALKING TOURS\n\nCentral Cape Town is considered safe to explore on foot – and it’s super convenient too! Hitting the pavement to wander the streets is recommended as one of the ideal ways to experience the Central City by day – although do bear in mind that your main worry will be trying not to overspend in the markets, shops and cafés. If you would like to add some historical background to your outing, you can find various applications at Voicemap.me and iTunes.\n\nCape Town Free Walking Tours\n\n076 636 9007 These free 1½-hour walking tours leave daily from Greenmarket Square – look out for the green umbrellas on the corner of Burg and Shortmarket streets. The 11am tour takes a historic amble around the\n\nCentral City, while the 2pm itinerary includes the Bo-Kaap neighbourhood. freewalkingtourscapetown.co.za\n\nFootsteps to Freedom\n\n083 452 1112/083 309 5378 These cultural and historical tours express the company’s philosophy that, behind Cape Town’s majestic mountains and vineyard vistas, lie the stories of local people. On their eponymous walking tour and Nelson Mandela themed walk, passionate guides narrate the CBD’s history on street corners and squares. They also offer private tours further afield. footstepstofreedom.co.za\n\nGood Hope Adventures\n\n082 482 4006 Good Hope Adventures’ Tunnel Tour explores the CBD’s secret subterranean rivers and canals, some of which date back to the 17th century. They once supplied the CBD and passing ships with fresh spring water, and today make a fascinating tour. They also offer other tours, including historical city walking tours and ghost and evening walks – creepy! Outings can be tailormade and groups are small with experienced guides. goodhopeadventures.com\n\nLook out for the CCID’s professional cleaners in their distinctive yellow uniforms.\n\n#KeepItClean\n\nGrand Parade & City Hall\n\n##### 10 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020 CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\nGARDENS, SQUARES, PUBLIC SPACES\n\nChurch Square P 19\n\nCnr Parliament and Spin streets Stop here to see the 11 granite blocks on the square that display the names of slaves traded here. Slaves would wait under the “slave tree” while their owners attended church services in Die Groote Kerk. Church Square is one of the oldest defined parcels of land around which the early town of Cape Town was established. The first building, a slave lodge, was built there in 1679 and Die Groote Kerk in 1701.\n\nArch for the Arch P 11\n\nEntry to the Company’s Garden, Government Avenue A tribute to one of the nation’s most beloved freedom fighters, Archbishop Emeritus Desmond Tutu, Arch for the Arch was commissioned by Design Indaba as a commemoration and celebration of the life and work of this great man, himself lovingly referred to simply as, ‘The Arch’. The installation was unveiled on 7 October 2017 coinciding with Tutu’s 86th birthday.\n\nDe Tuynhuys P 9\n\nThe Company’s Garden, Government Avenue Popular as a lunchtime chill spot for locals, there are many reasons to delight in this verdant park in the middle of the city. Take a meander through the tree-lined walkways filled with rubber and gingko trees and South Africa’s oldest tree: an ancient saffron pear tree. The South African National Gallery, Cape Town Holocaust and Genocide Centre and Iziko South African Museum are all located within the Company’s Garden, which was established to grow fresh fruits and vegetables for ships docking in the port en route to India.\n\nGrand Parade & City Hall P 18\n\nCnr Buitenkant and Darling streets Located next to the Castle of Good Hope, the Grand Parade was once used for military parades. This is where crowds gathered to listen to Nelson Mandela’s first public address on his release. His statue stands today on the steps of City Hall, to mark the occasion.\n\nGreenmarket Square P 10\n\nBetween Shortmarket and Longmarket streets, just off St Georges Mall With its craft stalls, cafés and Art Deco buildings, cobbled Greenmarket Square was created in 1696, originally as a fresh-produce market serving ships moored in Table Bay. Bustling with traders and browsing tourists today, the square is a fun place to buy craftwork and souvenirs from throughout Africa.\n\nHeritage Square P 2\n\nCnr Bree and Shortmarket streets This block of Cape Dutch, Georgian and Victorian buildings was rescued from becoming a parking garage, and now houses a variety of restaurants and bars. At the centre, the Heritage Square courtyard is a popular venue for after-work drinks.\n\nJetty Square P 13\n\nCnr Lower Long and Jetty streets Featuring skeletal shark sculptures by celebrated artist Ralph Borland, the square highlights the fact that the site was, before the Foreshore was developed in the 1960s, under water. It’s a spot where visitors can stop to reflect on nature even while walking through the CBD. Don’t be surprised when the sharks make a sound as you walk by – they interact with passersby via infrared sensors, and move in the wind. Freaky!\n\nLong Street Cape Town’s main drag is one of the Central City’s most vibrant streets, both during the day and at night, and is lined with boutiques, bars, craft shops, cafés and restaurants to suit all pockets and vibes. Grab a seat on the wrought-iron balcony of one of the Victorian-era buildings, which is the perfect spot for watching the scene unfolding below while sipping a drink or chowing down.\n\nOUT AND ABOUT\n\nDON’T LEAVE handbags under tables or on the backs of chairs.\n\nDON’T LEAVE cellphones or wallets on restaurant tables.\n\nKEEP YOUR credit card in sight all the time.\n\nDON’T LEAVE drinks unattended.\n\nvisit Visit capetownccid.org\n\nCLOSEST PARKING (SEE PAGE 63)\n\nWALLET FRIENDLY\n\nA TREAT\n\nSPECIAL OCCASION\n\nCHILD FRIENDLY\n\nWHEELCHAIR FRIENDLY P\n\nNorth Wharf P 4\n\nWharf Street, off Loop Street This square is hidden among hotels, including the upmarket Protea Hotel of the same name, near the harbour end of Bree Street. This is where where you will find some good restaurants and cafés that make quiet alternatives to the bustling eateries further up Long and Bree streets.\n\nPier Place P 14\n\nOff Heerengracht This square in the Foreshore area, which received a R2-million upgrade a few years ago, is a popular lunch spot for local office workers and executives. Mimicking the human activity in the vicinity, sculptor Egon Tania’s life-like statues of people playing, talking and reading the paper populate the square. Public spaces like this have been created to encourage Capetonians to reclaim the Central City.\n\nThibault Square P 13\n\nBetween Hans Strijdom Avenue and Riebeek, Long and Adderley streets, at the end of St Georges Mall This sizeable, bricked plaza is surrounded by high-rises; Thibault Square is one of the city’s favourite lunchtime relaxation spots and Cape\n\nTown’s version of the type of city square you would find all over Europe. Some of the city’s oldest and highest skyscrapers overlook the plaza, and there are numerous coffee shops, small restaurants and take-away outlets on its fringes. The bronze and steel sculpture on the western edge of the plaza by South African sculptor John Skotnes entitled Mythological Landscape, is a celebration of the diversity of this cosmopolitan city.\n\nPLACES OF INTEREST\n\nAfrica Centre P 11\n\n28 St Georges Mall, 021 418 3336 This African arts and culture NGO promotes pan-African culture, specifically as a tool for social change, and organises the Infecting the City public arts festival (infectingthecity.com). At its Talking Heads (talkingheads.org.za) events, specialists – from geophysicists to champion surfers – are invited to give presentations. Other programmes that celebrate what it means to be African in the 21st century frequently take place as well, so keep an eye on the website for details. africacentre.net\n\nSt Georges Mall A pedestrianised thoroughfare, St Georges Mall is home to the weekly Thursday Earth Fair Market as well as permanent kiosks and stalls. It runs northeast from St George’s Cathedral and passes near Greenmarket Square. It’s a picturesque conduit through the CBD, with benches, art and cafés.\n\nSt Georges Mall\n\n##### 12 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020 CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\nCape Town International Convention Centre (CTICC) P 8\n\nConvention Square, 1 Lower \nLong Street, 021 410 5000 \nContaining artworks and impressive spaces \nsuch as the 2 000m 2 Grand Ballroom, \nthe CTICC plays host to a number of major \nevents. Notable programmes include \nDecorex Cape Town and the Good Food \n& Wine Show (goodfoodandwineshow.co.za) \nas well as a variety of conferences and \nmusical performances. \ncticc.co.za \n\nCentral Library P 18\n\nOld Drill Hall, cnr Parade and Darling streets, 021 444 0983 Built in the late 1800s, this building was originally where soldiers drilled during bad weather. Today, it’s a bustling library with something for everyone. Events are hosted here and books in all 11 official South African languages as well as newspapers and magazines about the Central City that date back more than 20 years are stocked. capetown.gov.za/library\n\nCentre for the Book P 9\n\n62 Queen Victoria Street, 021 423 2669 Occupying a beautiful domed Edwardian building, this centre, run by the National Library of South Africa, promotes reading and writing by way of events such as book discussions, launches, poetry readings and writing workshops. It also houses the Children’s Reading Centre, which provides opportunities for children up until the age of seven to read and write their own stories. There are two rooms, accommodating from 80 to 300 people, available for hire. centerforthebook.org.za\n\nHouses of Parliament P 19\n\n120 Plein Street, 021 403 2266 This imposing red-and-white building consists of three sections completed at different periods, the original in the 1800s. It has witnessed many pivotal moments in South African history, including the assassination of former prime minister Hendrik Verwoerd, the so-called architect of apartheid, in 1966. Call to arrange a free guided tour on weekdays. parliament.gov.za\n\nLong Street Baths P 9\n\nCnr Long and Orange streets, 021 422 0100 Hidden away inside a gorgeous Art Nouveau building is the Long Street bathhouse, which was built in 1908. There are Turkish baths, a magnificent 25m heated pool as well as a children’s pool. Be sure to call ahead if you\n\nIziko South African National Gallery P 10\n\nThe Company’s Garden, Government Avenue, 021 481 3970 One of South Africa’s best art museums, the National Gallery’s permanent collection includes paintings, photography, sculptures, beadwork and textiles from across Africa and Europe. iziko.org.za\n\nIziko South African National Gallery\n\nvisit Visit capetownccid.org\n\nCLOSEST PARKING (SEE PAGE 63)\n\nWALLET FRIENDLY\n\nA TREAT\n\nSPECIAL OCCASION\n\nCHILD FRIENDLY\n\nWHEELCHAIR FRIENDLY P\n\nCapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID wish to use the Turkish baths, which have a sauna and steam room with massages available. The baths are enjoyed by athletes, locals and tourists and this is the perfect spot to spend a relaxing afternoon. The pool is reserved for use by women between 10am and 2pm on Tuesdays, and lifeguards are on duty. capetown.gov.za\n\nOpen House\n\nCnr Long and Dorp streets This bright-red, 10.5m-high house installation, the winning concept in a Western Cape Government public art competition, was constructed by artist Jacques Coetzer. A symbol of democracy and diversity, its balconies host open mic poetry, talks and performances, and encourage interaction between people from across society to celebrate freedom of speech. All forms of music, comedy, theatre, exhibitions, art and play are encouraged at this bustling location in the city. westerncape.gov.za/general publication/open-house-public art-competition\n\nZip Zap Circus P 24\n\nFounder’s Garden, Jan Smuts Street, 021 421 8623 Zip Zap Circus is a well-established social circus school that was founded in Cape Town in 1992, with the intention to inspire young people and help build a new culture of peaceful coexistence in South Africa. It is widely recognised as a major contributor to tourism and its main focus is on helping children to go after their dreams, while at the same time supporting the circus arts in South Africa. Zip Zap’s programmes are free to all participants and they receive financial and material support from various organistions, charities, corporations and foundations. Check their website for upcoming events and tours. The dome can be hired for special functions. zip-zap.co.za\n\nP 10\n\nGALLERIES\n\nThe African Portrait P 11\n\nCnr Long and Hout streets, \n021 426 1886 \nThis gallery specialises in representations \nof Africa’s many faces, from Zulu chiefs and \nMaasai warriors to Saharan women and \nSamburu elders. It offers limited-edition prints \non canvas and represents two local artists, \nGrant Oxche and Mekhala van der Schyff. \ntheafricanportrait.com \n\nAssociation for Visual Arts (AVA) Gallery P 10\n\n35 Church Street, 021 424 7436 \nOne of the country’s oldest non-profit \ngalleries, AVA showcases contemporary \nSouth African art in all media. The gallery \nhas been in operation in various guises since \n1971, promoting visual arts and introducing \nrising stars. \nava.co.za \n\nThe Cape Gallery P 10\n\n60 Church Street, 021 423 5309 This Church Street stalwart, surrounded by colourful Georgian and Dutch buildings, specialises in South African scenes, from landscapes to street art. The conservative artworks act as reports of the flora and fauna of the area. Vibrant street art has recently added some African flair to the gallery. capegallery.co.za\n\nEbony P 2\n\n67 Loop Street, 021 424 9985 Besides breathtaking art, Ebony sells design savvy furniture and accessories, including lighting, jewellery and even toys. On the walls you’ll find photos, prints and paintings, with some particularly striking images of Beninese muscle men. The gallery focuses on contemporary and modern African art and represents a core group of established and emerging talent. ebonycurated.com\n\n4 ways you can donate to the CCID’s partner NGOs, rather than giving directly on the streets\n\n1 SMS “GIVE” to 38088 to donate R10*. 2 Download the free SnapScan app onto your smartphone and scan the code above to donate the amount of your choice**. * Our partner NGOs receive an average of R8 depending on your cellphone service provider. ** A 3% admin fee goes to SnapScan. 3 Pay with Paypal. Go to paypal.me/CCIDShowYouCare. 4 Make an EFT payment to the Show You Care campaign. You can find bank details at showyoucare.co.za. 5 Or you can make a donation directly to the NGO of your choice. All items in good condition are appreciated, especially tinned food, toiletries (eg sanitary towels), socks, gloves and beanies. The CCID’s six primary partner NGOs are The Homestead, Ons Plek, Youth Solutions Africa, The Hope Exchange, Khulisa Social Solutions and Straatwerk. Visit capetownccid.org/about-ccid/ partners for more info.\n\nvisit Visit capetownccid.org\n\ni\n\nYOU NEED TO KNOW\n\nMake your time in Cape Town count by getting your head around these local South\n\n| Africanisms that | | |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| could make you | RED! The store boasts two floors of novels and nonfiction, the staff are really clued-up and as an added bonus there is a coffee shop and lounge. Regular events include the popular Saturday morning children’s story time (at 11am), perfect for kids between the ages of three and eight. For the adults, there are regular evening book launches. booklounge.co.za\n\nClarke’s Bookshop P 5\n\n199 Long Street, 021 423 5739 \nA legendary city book store, Clarke's Bookshop \nis renowned for its collection of Africana. \nFrom antiquarian to ANC memoirs, Clarke’s \nspecialises in books on southern Africa, \nparticularly new tomes. Having celebrated its \n60th birthday in 2016, Clarke’s continues to \nensure its two floors of shelves and tables hold \ntreasures for collectors and holiday readers \nalike. Pop in for new, secondhand and \nout-of-print books. \nclarkesbooks.co.za \n\nCRAFTS & GIFTS\n\nAfrican Craft Market P 2\n\n62 Long Street Whether you are on the hunt for jewellery, wooden sculptures or even beaded bowls, this dainty shop, packed to the rafters with African curios, souvenirs and gifting options should have it. Engage with the friendly stall owners and find out more about where their products come from. Here, you’ll find perfect gifts and mementos of your African holiday.\n\nThe Long Street Antique Market P 10\n\n127 Long Street, 021 423 2666 Roll up, roll up and get them here: coins, medals, militaria, antiques, collectables, books, jewellery and more. This quaint arcade of 12 shops is a great place for browsing. theantiquearcade.co.za\n\nYoungblood Africa Arts & Culture Development P 2\n\nBeautifull Life Building, 70-72 Bree Street, 021 424 0074 This arts foundation supports emerging South African artists by offering them capital\n\nMerchants on Long\n\nP 11\n\n34 Long Street, 021 422 2828 Merchants on Long is a store house of well-curated luxury and hand made brands that are all sourced and made in Africa. And if you want to make something up yourself, you can also buy exclusive hand-printed fabric by the metre. carolenevin.com\n\nChandler House P 2\n\n53 Church Street, 021 424 4810 This Georgian building houses the Voorkamer Gallery, where you can see local artists’ work, and a delightful shop stocking beautifully curated items from locally made homeware and linen to unique stationery and bespoke ceramics. chandlerhouse.co.za\n\nMoroccan Warehouse P 25\n\nCnr Commercial and Buitenkant streets, 021 461 8318 This treasure trove sells the fruits of the owners’ annual buying trips to Marrakesh, Fez and Tangier. The stock includes décor, carpets, mosaic tables, mint tea glasses and scatter cushions – all guaranteed to give your home some Maghrebi mystique. facebook.com/Moroccan-Warehouse\n\nNewport Lighting Concepts and Design P 3\n\n19 Bree Street, 021 447 0049 Owner Guy Harris graduated from Design Time School of Interior Design in 1992 and has worked in the industry ever since. Newport.co.za\n\nMungo P 2\n\n78 Hout Street, 021 201 2374 Since 1998, the folks at Mungo have been weaving quality, natural-fibre homeware textiles at their mill in Plettenberg Bay. At their store on Hout Street you’ll find the full range of Mungo products as well as their inner-city Micro Mill, where contemporary textile design meets traditional weaving processes on a 100-year-old Hattersley loom. mungo.co.za\n\nStable South African Design Collective P 2\n\n4th floor, Valuta House, 74 Shortmarket Street, 087 150 6538 Stable is a platform that showcases the best of innovative local design with more than 115 SA designers and brands under the Stable umbrella. Stable is based in the CBD but you can also shop online with local and international door-to-door shipping offered. Stable.org.za\n\nSkinny laMinx P 5\n\n201 Bree Street, 021 424 6290 Designer Heather Moore’s work includes fabrics, decorative items, stationery and placemats, all made in Cape Town and sold locally and internationally. Thebigbox.co.za\n\nCape Cobra Leathercraft P 3\n\n43 Bree Street, 021 415 3440 A third generation family business that was established in 1972 by German-born founder Lothar Schäfer, Cape Cobra is a major exporter of exotic leather goods made from the skins of sustainably farmed Nile crocodile, python, ostrich and python. (opposite FNB Bank) 021 422 3186 UNLIMITED BUFFET UNLIMITED DRINKS\n\nFood Inn India www.foodinnindia.co.za\n\nCLAREMONT 156 Long Street, Cape Town 021 422 5060\n\nEATERIES IN 100 OUR GUIDE +\n\n### EATERIES IN OUR GUIDE100\n\n+\n\nFOR MORE PLACES, VISIT WWW.CAPETOWNCCID.ORG\n\nBEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY\n\n##### 28 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020 CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\nAFRICAN\n\nAddis in Cape P 10\n\nCnr Long and Church streets, 021 424 5722 Ethiopian cuisine is a culinary delight with its thick and spicy stews served on injera, a pancake-like sourdough flatbread. Addis in Cape offers these exotic East African dishes and homemade Ethiopian coffee to finish the feast, in an authentic setting that includes woven, basket-like mesop tables. addisincape.co.za\n\nGlobal Tribes P 25\n\n58 Commercial Street, 021 461 1552 If you're in the mood for authentic African fare, this is a good inner-city option. Eat in or call and order for delivery.\n\nLucy P 5\n\n281 Long Street, 021 422 1797 Foodies seeking an authentic Ethopian taste experience will not be disappointed by the offering at Lucy, which is as warmly real as it gets. Vegans are also catered for in this interesting establishment.\n\nMadam Taitou P 12\n\n77 Long Street and cnr Wale and Buitengracht streets, 021 426 6969 / 072 704 6182 Specialising in Ethiopian cuisine, this gem boasts furniture draped in animal skins in a treehouse-styled space that offers guests privacy and a sense of gastronomical excitement. Try the Kifto: lean beef cooked in spiced butter and served with cottage cheese and salad on flatbread, is a firm favourite. Or opt for the Sergenya, a platter containing samples of all the dishes on the menu. madamtaitou@gmail.com\n\nMama Africa P 5\n\n178 Long Street, 021 424 8634 With live marimba every night, this local institution creates sunny African vibes alongside dishes from across the continent. Mamaafricarestaurant.co.za\n\nMariams Kitchen\n\n31 Heerengracht, 021 421 3636 P 14\n\n101 St Georges Mall, 021 423 0772 P 10\n\nThis is a great (halaal) place to try Cape Malay cuisine, a uniquely Cape Town mix of African,\n\nActive Sushi\n\nP 4\n\n2-4 Bree Street, \n021 418 0064 \nRun by sushi \nlovers for sushi \naficionados, \nthis casual eatery \nis a fun spot for \nyour Japanese fix. \nLook out for their \nall-you-can-eat \nbuffet. \nactivesushi. \ncom \n\nActive Sushieat Visit capetownccid.org\n\nCLOSEST PARKING (SEE PAGE 63)\n\nWALLET FRIENDLY\n\nA TREAT\n\nSPECIAL OCCASION\n\nCHILD FRIENDLY\n\nWHEELCHAIR FRIENDLY P\n\nAsian and European influences. Mariams is a Cape Town institution.\n\nMeeting Point P 11\n\n67 Strand Street, 021 422 1073 Get a taste of Tanzania at Meeting Point, a modern Swahili restaurant that's big on friendliness and home-style cooking. Tanzani ans themselves dine here.\n\nASIAN\n\nGalbi P 5\n\n210 Long Street, 021 424 3030 At this “Korean fusion barbecue” every table has a small grill on which diners braai their meat, chicken and vegetables. galbi.co.za\n\nHaiku P 10\n\n58 Burg Street, 021 424 7000 Take a tour through East Asia in this intimate, elegant setting. Modern Asian dishes are served tapas-style (think steamed or fried dim sum, stir-fry dishes, miso soup and sushi) and there's an open kitchen. haikurestaurant.com\n\nNuri Sushi Factory P 18\n\n8 Parliament Street, 021 461 8719 An \"Africanised\" sushi bar, this eatery serves gourmet interpretations of sushi, including spicy seared tuna roses and salmon skin rolls. nuri.co.za\n\nObi P 13\n\n14 Long Street, 021 418 4040 Papa San, chef of the former Takumi in Park Road, serves quality Japanese food with staples like sushi, tempura and ramen on the menu. Look out for the lunch specials, which often include a bento box and tonakstu set. obirestaurant.co.za\n\nThe Sea Palace P 4\n\n2 Long Street, 021 418 0961 Originally at the entrance to the V&A Waterfront, this Chinese establishment has found a new home. The dishes are as delicious as ever.\n\nSimply Asia P 2\n\n96 Shortmarket Street, 021 426 4347 This much-loved Thai chain offers three levels of spicy heat – blinking (mild), brilliant (medium) and shooting stars (hot). simplyasia.co.za\n\nSouth China Dim Sum Bar P 5\n\n289 Long Street, 078 846 3656 Enjoy Cantonese tapas of dumplings, rice noodle rolls, vegetables, meat and more, served in steamer baskets or on small plates. facebook.com/SouthChinaDimSumBar\n\nTomo P 2\n\n86 Loop Street, 021 422 0635 The sushi is what keeps customers returning to this Japanese restaurant, but the miso soup, beef tataki and dumplings are also delicious. facebook.com/tomocapetown\n\nYu Restaurant P 14\n\n57 Heerengracht Street, 021 111 0452 Located in the Onyx Apartment Hotel building, Yu is open to the public and hotel guests and serves Asian cuisine, sushi, tapas, dim sum and more, with contemporary flair. newmarkhotels.com/places/in house/mrkt/\n\nBAKERIES & COFFEE HOUSES\n\nBean There P 10\n\n58 Wale Street, 087 943 2228 If you’re going to have a cuppa in pretty\n\nCopacabana - pg 33\n\nGlobal Tribes - pg 28\n\nMadam Taitou - pg 28\n\nSouth China Dim Sum Bar\n\n##### 30 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020 CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\nsurroundings, why not make it a certified Fairtrade one? There’s a selection of cakes and tarts to accompany your caffeine fix. beanthere.co.za\n\nBetween Us P 2\n\n176 Bree Street, 021 823 8695 \nA stunning spot for coffee, a healthy breakfast \nbowl or lunch made with love. The flower \narrangements, stonewalls and mountain views \nwill finish the experience. \nbetweenus.capetown \n\nBlu Bean Café P 10\n\nMandela Rhodes Place, \n136 St Georges Mall \nStart your day with a value-for-money \nbreakfast on St Georges Mall. There’s an \nextensive à la carte and buffet menu, and you \ncan end your day with a cocktail. \n\nCharly’s Bakery P 26\n\n38 Canterbury Street, 021 461 5181 \nThis well-known bakery creates cakes \nresembling everything from guitars to \nhelicopters. Pop in for a cupcake and \nappreciate the happy vibes. \ncharlysbakery.co.za \n\nCoffee Zone P 19\n\n75 Plein Street, 021 462 6004 This super-popular coffee spot is where all the locals in the area get their morning fix, so be prepared for a bustling vibe. Stay for a reasonably priced breakfast as well.\n\nConways Café Society P 25\n\n79 Roeland Street, 021 461 1527 Coffee is the first order of the day at Conways, followed closely by wraps, sandwiches, pizza and pasta. Watch the world go by from the venue’s big windows overlooking the street.\n\nCrust Café P 6\n\n243 Bree Street, 021 412 2222 This art café is open for breakfast and lunch,\n\nMonday to Saturday and offers a delivery service, within walking distance, for busy deskbound patrons. They offer daily specials, a soup of the day, as well as platters for corporate events. The all-day breakfast is a winner or you can build your own breakfast from a wide selection of ingredients. Crust.co.za\n\nThe Crema P 13\n\n9 Long Street, 076 452 4470 Tucked away on the ground floor of a corporate building is the perfect coffee pit stop to refuel your day. You can pick up a toastie, choc-chip biscuit or muffin too. the-crema.business.site\n\nDeluxe Coffeeworks P 10\n\n25 Church Street, 072 569 9579 Its beans are brewed by discerning establishments and roasting is the main business here, but you can also grab a cup (or buy a bag) in a cool environment. You’ll find Deluxe’s coffee in various spots in the CBD. deluxecoffeeworks.co.za\n\nEcco il Caffe P 30\n\nThe Helix, Rua Vasco de Gama, Foreshore, 021 418 0428 This small franchise is the perfect lunchtime\n\nSmak - pg 32\n\neat Visit capetownccid.org\n\nCLOSEST PARKING (SEE PAGE 63)\n\nWALLET FRIENDLY\n\nA TREAT\n\nSPECIAL OCCASION\n\nCHILD FRIENDLY\n\nWHEELCHAIR FRIENDLY P\n\nCapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID spot. Grab a cup of coffee, a slice of cake, or an artisan sandwich, mouthwatering wrap, or fresh and wholesome salad. eccoilcaffe.co.za\n\nGiulio’s Café P 3\n\n16 Loop Street, 021 418 6304 \nWhat a pleasure to visit this cheerful, award \nwinning Italian eatery, where chef-patron \nGiulio is enthusiastic and the food is delicious. \nGiulio worked under Jamie Oliver and has \nItalian roots, so you know his gnocchi, penne, \npanino di Parma and all-day breakfasts are \ntrue delights. \ngiulios.co.za \n\nHaas Collective P 26\n\n19 Buitenkant Street, 021 461 1812 Haas serves attention-grabbing coffees, including the “death wish” (the strongest on Earth, apparently) and kopi luwak (which consists of coffee cherries that are partly digested and defecated by a small mammal called the Asian palm civet). haascollective.com\n\nHonest Chocolate Café P 10\n\n64 Wale Street, 076 765 8306 There’s no reason to deny yourself a sweet treat every now and again, and again and again, even if you’re generally health conscious. This is the ideal spot to finish off a lunch or bring an early dinner to an end, with a selection of chocolate truffles, tarts and cakes made with pure cacao from which to choose. Many options are vegan-friendly and dairy-free, including dairy-free ice cream and milkshakes. Plus, you can pop in at the “not-so-secret” secret gin bar at the back. honestchocolate.co.za\n\nJ&M Biltong Café P 3\n\n36 Waterkant Street There are few things South Africans love more than biltong. But J&M is about so much more: you can get yourself a banting and caffeine fix too. Choose from cured meats, nuts and baked confectionery (okay, that’s not banting) while ordering an espresso.\n\nKamili\n\nP 10 Cnr 29 Shortmarket and \nLong streets, \nP 26 48 Harrington Street, \n060 864 2941 \nThis is the brainchild of coffee connoisseur \nTheo Snyckers, who roasts beans and \nintroduces drinkers to new flavours. The \nemphasis is on great coffee – any way you \nwant it, in any of the two chilled venues. \nkamilicoffee.co.za \n\nNew York Bagels P 26\n\n44 Harrington Street, 021 007 4315 The morning sunshine lights up this corner hub where hipsters gather for a pre-work coffee. Its top-notch bagels promise a lunch queue most days, so come early.\n\nRCaffé P 10\n\n138 Long Street, 021 424 1124 RCaffé serves top-rated coffee and imaginative fare with more than a nod to healthy eating. From breakfast to cocktails, this is the perfect spot in one of the most\n\nHaas Collective\n\npopular streets in the CBD. It also offers \na special banting menu. \nrcaffe.biz \n\nSmak P 3\n\n22 Bree Street, 021 418 3266 A coffee bar-cum-patisserie with a deli offering cold meats and cheeses, plus freshly baked breads, Smak is a popular addition to the CBD’s eatery options. Create your own cheese-and-cold cut platter, or try the Asian broth or a classic French croque monsieur. smak.co.za\n\nStarlings at Labotessa P 2\n\n5 Church Square, 021 010 6600 Exquisite coffee and delectable breakfasts are served all day at Starlings. Lunchtime treats on the small but enticing menu include sandwiches, tarts and wraps.\n\nSwan Café P 25\n\nCnr Buitenkant and Barrack streets, \n079 454 4758 \nSweet and savoury crepes are this \nParisian-themed café’s pièce de résistance, \nand there’s a great tea menu too. Plus, there’s \ngood news for the gluten-freebies – you can \nhave your pancake and eat it! \nswancafe.co.za \n\nTruth Coffee P 25\n\n36 Buitenkant Street, 021 200 0440 MSN Travel and the Daily Telegraph declared this café, with its ornate metalwork and baristas dressed for the Mad Hatter’s tea party, the world’s best coffee shop. truthcoffee.com\n\nYours Truly\n\nP 5 63 Loop Street, 081 504 0064, \nP 11 175 Long Street, 021 422 3788 \nCoffee, sandwiches and pizza are available \nthrough the takeaway hatch or in the black \nand-white interior. \nyourstrulycafe.co.za \n\nVelvet Cake Co. P 3\n\n16 Loop Street, 021 914 0482 Look no further if you're craving great cake, be it a slice or a whole celebration cake. Locals would recommend the rainbow, carrot and red velvet cakes, but there’s really so much to choose from, you’ll have to visit more than once. thevelvetcakeco.com\n\nWimpy P 10\n\nCnr St Georges Mall and Church Street, 021 424 3470 The first Wimpy opened in the ‘60s and has become a South African institution. Grab a signature Wimpy coffee and an all-day breakfast at this popular branch.\n\nBISTROS, CAFÉS & TAPAS BARS\n\n&Bikes Tours, Café and Cycle Boutique P 11\n\n32 Loop Street, 021 823 8790 The CBD is perfect for cycling. Rent a bike and then stay for a coffee or a light lunch. Wraps and sandwiches are the order of the day, and there’s free Wi-Fi too. andbikes.co.za\n\nBouchon Bistro P 2\n\n95 Hout Street, 021 422 0695 This tapas restaurant, housed in an urban wine cellar (home of Dorrance wines), offers a sophisticated yet relaxed food and wine experience in a cosy setting. bouchon.co.za\n\nBrownies & downieS P 13\n\n2 Long Street, 021 200 0459 Light meals, salads and good coffee are on the menu at this spot that started in the Netherlands. Staffed by people with special needs, it's part of the NPO Our Second Home. browniesdownies.co.za eat Visit capetownccid.org\n\nWHEELCHAIR FRIENDLY P\n\nCLOSEST PARKING (SEE PAGE 63)\n\nWALLET FRIENDLY\n\nA TREAT\n\nSPECIAL OCCASION\n\nCHILD FRIENDLY\n\nKleinsky’s Delicatessen\n\nCafé Frank P 5\n\nKleinsky’s Delicates sen\n\nbeautiful garden setting. There is a selection of dishes on offer: from light meals to wholesome breakfast, teatime favourites as well as traditional South African fare. thecompanysgarden.com\n\n160 Bree Street, 062 667 4633 “Honest food for working folk” is the motto here; in this instance the workers tend to be from the legal fraternity (the High Court is nearby). The menu is simple and ingredients are, as far as possible, organically sourced. cafefrank.com\n\nCopacabana P 5\n\n32 Parliament \nStreet, \n082 583 4162 \nIt’s all about the \nbagel at this ode \nto a New York deli. \nSmoked salmon, \npastrami and \nlamb BLT are \nsome of the menu \noptions. \nkleinskys.co.za \n\nCafé Sante P 10\n\n13 Bloem Street, 083 240 2005 Open until 2am, this casual spot has a different vibe depending on the time of day. Enjoy smoothies for breakfast or nachos at nighttime.\n\n7 Shortmarket Street, 021 426 2939 This café on iconic Greenmarket Square serves light fare and is the perfect place to stop after shopping at the market.\n\nFolk Café P 4\n\nCafé Riteve P 9\n\n7 Bree Street, 021 276 1795 Its philosophy (“Essentially, eating and drinking is a cultural experience”) is embodied in a welcoming environment with a generous, all-day menu. folkcoffee.co.za\n\n88 Hatfield Street, 021 465 1594 This café at the South African Jewish Museum offers kosher food, with everything from falafel to pizza on the menu. Take a form of identification and your residential address with you to gain access. caferiteve.co.za\n\nFood Lover’s Market Cafés\n\nP 10 122 St Georges Mall, 021 424 0294\n\n| The Company’s Garden | P | 22 Standard Bank, 44 Hertzog |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Restaurant P | 9 Boulevard, Foreshore | |\n| 19 Queen Victoria Street, | P | 13 Icon Building, cnr Loop Street |\n| 021 423 2919 | and Hans Strijdom Avenue | |\n| This old-school tearoom with a contemporary | Part of the Food Lover’s Market group, | |\n| touch has for years welcomed families to its | these venues offer a sit-down opportunity, | |\n| CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID | 2020 | BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 33 |\n\n##### 34 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020 CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\nwith varied dishes, such as croissants, pasta, \nsushi and grilled calamari. \nfruitandvegcity.co.za \n\nFork P 2\n\n84 Long Street, 021 424 6334 \nFork offers a distinctive take on tapas: the \ndishes, each containing four pieces, are \ndesigned for sharing. \nfork-restaurants.co.za \n\nHouse of H P 10\n\n112 Loop Street, 076 699 6146 This innovative, homely restaurant now boasts a hearty menu with salads, burgers, steak and more as well as a barbershop and coffee bar. houseofh.co.za\n\nThe Ghibli Bar and Pool Terrace P 13\n\nRadisson Blu Hotel & Residence, 22 Riebeek Street, 021 467 4000 If you’re looking for a swanky location to enjoy a drink but want a bite to eat with it, take the elevator to the terrace of the Radisson Blu. radissonblu.com/en/hotel-cape town-residence/bars\n\nLa Parada P 2\n\n107 Bree Street, 021 426 0330 This bar serves Spain’s favourite nibbles. The open-fronted space also boasts street seating. laparada.co.za\n\nLola’s P 5\n\n228 Long Street, 021 423 0885 \nThis bistro has a regularly changing \nblackboard menu. \nlolas.co.za \n\nLunchworks P 3\n\n22 Waterkant Street, 021 418 3118 Lunchworks serves sandwiches, salads, wraps, bagels, burgers and organic coffee. lunchworks.co.za\n\nManjas P 11\n\nHoliday Inn, 66 Strand Street, 021 431 7500 Located in a 19-floor hotel, Manjas is open 24 hours and has exquisite views of Signal Hill and Table Mountain. Its trendy décor also showcases some amazing artworks.\n\nMax Bagels P 2\n\n120 Bree Street, 076 042 0224 This New York-style bagel bar serves Rosetta Roastery coffee, pressed juices and great bagels. Try classic fillings like salt beef, pickle and mustard, or inventive ones such as roast cauliflower, grilled Emmenthal and bacon. maxbagels.com\n\nMink & Trout P 5\n\n127 Bree Street, 021 426 2534 Open for lunch Monday to Saturday and dinner Tuesday to Saturday, this is just the spot for a tapas lunch or weeknight dinner. The tapas menu includes dishes such as whitebait with lemon and dill mayonnaise and arancini with mushrooms and cauliflower. Main courses cater for all tastes and include dishes that hero mussels, fish, gnocchi and Elgin pork belly. You can expect friendly service and a pleasing selection of wine, beer, cider and soft drinks. The open-plan show kitchen and floor-to-ceiling wine rack add a touch of class, but enjoying the fare here is an informal experience overall. tajhotels.com\n\n33 Waterkant Street, 021 418 3020 With an ambiance both rustic and French, Nomad serves all-day breakfasts, tasting platters, burgers and flatbreads. facebook.com/nomadbistrofusion\n\nLa Tête - pg 37\n\nOlami P 5\n\nMotherland P 10\n\nMandela Rhodes Place, Cnr St Georges Mall and Wale Street, 021 424 8570 This spot brings a folksy feel to the glass and-steel structure that is Mandela Rhodes Place. As well as good coffee, sandwiches and muffins, it sells beans by the bag, plus a selection of souvenirs and accessories. motherlandcoffee.co.za\n\n231 Bree Street, 021 424 7480 Wholesome, delicious food is the stock in trade of this popular lunchtime venue primarily for takeaways although there are some inside and outside seating options for patrons who want to linger. You can choose your own combinations to create a gorgeous lunch from the variety of vegetables, salads and protein such as meatballs, curries and stews. The variety of freshly squeezed juice is always a temptation. facebook.com/ Olami-549503688551991/\n\nRiverine Rabbit - pg 38\n\nMozart on Church P 10\n\nThe Raptor Room P 25\n\n37 Church Street, 021 422 1765 Delightfully situated in the art-gallery precinct of the pedestrian stretch of Church Street, Mozart on Church is an invitingly relaxed spot. A generous menu spans all-day breakfasts and gourmet sandwiches to a lunchtime salad buffet.\n\n79 Roeland Street, 087 625 0630 Funky and colourful, this cheerful spot comes with a menu that spans options from wood fired Portuguese loaf sandwiches to salads of bacon and Karoo figs.\n\nMelt Café P 5\n\nRaw Espresso Bar P 19\n\nSkinny Legs Luxury Café - pg 36\n\n233 Long Street, 021 422 1603 \nSometimes bliss is as simple as a toasted \nsarmie. Melt has cottoned onto this comfort \nfood and taken it a step further. Think streaky \nbacon, syrup and Nutella. \nmeltcafe.co.za \n\n1 Mostert Street, 021 462 7986 Capetonians have embraced the burgeoning coffee culture with enthusiasm; the latest spot to fulfil our caffeine craving is this brick-lined haven serving strong, hot, well-priced coffee in a laid-back environment.\n\nMilk & Honey P 4\n\nRock’n Toast P 29\n\n42 Hans Strijdom Avenue, Foreshore, 087 470 0160 The Parisian décor gets a well-earned 10 out of 10. Appetisers such as tempura prawns, hoisin chicken wings and truffle oil fries grab\n\nMartin Hammerschlag Way, Foreshore, 021 425 4839 Nestled between car dealerships on the Foreshore is a laid-back spot for burgers\n\nTortilla Modern Mexican - pg 36\n\n##### 36 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020 CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\nand pastas. Great for a workday lunch with colleagues or an after-work nibble and beer.\n\nSeaBreeze Fish and Shell P 5\n\n213 Bree Street, 074 793 9349 This cool-blue spot with oyster- and cocktail bars and an outside-dining (read: people-watching) counter is a particularly welcome addition to the eating scene. Its Scottish owners say they want “each dish and experience to remind people what great seafood is all about”. Hear, hear!\n\nSkinny Legs Luxury Café P 2\n\n70 Loop Street, 021 423 5403 This “luxury café” is a cultured spot to enjoy wholesome food, along with some treats such as cakes, croissants and babycinos. You can watch as your food is freshly prepared in the bustling open plan kitchen. skinnylegsandall.co.za\n\nSouk P 5\n\n163 Long Street, 021 422 1404 \nA tasty selection of tapas and wide selection \nof the finest champagnes can all be enjoyed \non the picturesque terrace. Take note of their \nhalf-price tapas and cocktail special from \n3–6pm on Tuesdays to Fridays, and after-dark \nparty vibe. \nsoukcpt.com \n\nThe Station P 5\n\n207 Bree Street, 021 422 0566 The Station brings you a little taste of the London underground. Wash down a burger or tapas with one of the 11 signature cocktails named after London tube stations. thestation.capetown\n\nTortilla Modern Mexican P 13\n\nIcon Building, cnr Lower Long Street and Hans Strijdom Avenue, 021 418 4599 A lively lunchtime burrito bar where you can eat your fill of Mexican-influenced fare. The usual suspects are here – guacamole, black beans, salsa, jalapeños and tacos. facebook.com/TortillaModernMexican\n\nBURGERS\n\nCraft Burger Bar P 1\n\n63 Buitengracht, 021 424 0082 Build your own burger with a full, half or no bun, and finish it off with an assortment of delicious toppings. Pair it with a beer from the wide selection of local craft beers. craftburgerbar.co.za\n\nRoyale Eatery P 5\n\n273 Long Street, 021 422 4536 A must-visit Cape Town institution with over 50 burger variations and milkshakes. And their sweet potato fries never disappoint. royaleeatery.com\n\nDINERS\n\nBurger & Lobster P 2\n\n105 Bree Street, 021 422 4297 The clue to the menu is in the name: your choices are a beef burger, a lobster roll or just lobster! Skinny fries and a side salad complete the lineup. facebook.com/burgerlobsterSA\n\nClarke’s Bar & Dining Room 133 Bree Street, 087 470 0165 P 2 This diner attracts hungry hipsters with Nutella croissants, Caesar salad, pulled pork, oyster chowder and more. Portions are big, almost everything is handmade and, where possible, produce is free-range and organic. clarkesdining.co.za\n\nLa Muse P 6\n\n14 Jetty St, Circa Hotel, Foreshore, 021 431 8820 Situated on the ground floor of a luxury apartment hotel, the restaurant offers an\n\nTASTY CITY BOWLS\n\nMarrow\n\nP 2\n\n83 Loop Street, 079 556 3647 This culinary gem serves some of the best broths around. Try these for starters.\n\nTHE CLEAR BROTH chicken, chorizo, paprika and spring onions. Super delicious.\n\nTHE BROWN BROTH Turkish apricots, flaked almonds, lentils, baby marrow and venison. Aromatic and hearty.\n\nTHE WHITE BROTH A vegetable and coconut base, lemon grass, roasted aubergine, tofu and turnip. Perfect for vegans.\n\neat Visit capetownccid.org\n\nCLOSEST PARKING (SEE PAGE 63)\n\nWALLET FRIENDLY\n\nA TREAT\n\nSPECIAL OCCASION\n\nCHILD FRIENDLY\n\nWHEELCHAIR FRIENDLY P\n\nCapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID à la carte menu with traditional meat and seafood dishes. Expect some creative plating. circahotel.co.za/la-muse\n\nLefty’s P 26\n\n105 Harrington Street, 021 461 0407 This self-proclaimed dive bar, with its old pianos, graffiti and canteen tables, is great for pork ribs, burgers and fried chicken waffles.\n\nLong Street Café P 5 259 Long Street, 021 424 2464/2485 Nachos, club sandwiches, chicken wings, meat platters – that’s the food vibe. This is the spot to meet for a drink and a snack.\n\nSotano P 5\n\n199 Bree Street, 021 422 0567 You can find just about anything on the Mediterranean-inspired menu. The breakfasts are renowned and later you can relax with a glass of wine, sushi and live music. sotano.co.za\n\nThe Sea Street Bar & Lounge P 13\n\nRadisson Blu Hotel & Residence, 22 Riebeek Street, 021 467 4000 Escape the hustle and bustle of the street by enjoying a ligh snack at this modern hotel. radissonblu.com/en/hotel-cape town-residence/bars\n\nTiger’s Milk P 11\n\n44 Long Street, 021 422 3556 Part of a successful franchise, the menu features gourmet burgers, steaks and pizza. tigersmilk.co.za\n\nVixi Social House P 3\n\n49 Bree Street, 021 418 1338 Offering an all-day Mediterranean menu and coffee that's freshly roasted on the premises, this is an exciting new addition to lively Bree\n\nStreet, and is open for breakfast, lunch and dinner. facebook.com/vixisocialhouse/\n\nGOURMET\n\nChefs Warehouse and Canteen P 2\n\n92 Bree Street, 021 442 0128 Savour celebrated chef Liam Tomlin’s contemporary tapas offering at this popular dining spot in the middle of the CBD. Dishes change often as the focus is on fresh, local and sustainable ingredients and there is always a set menu of small sharing plates in three courses. Vegetarian options are always available and because of the casual nature of the venue, no bookings are taken. The oysters and legendary lemon posset are, however, worth queuing for. Chefswarehouse.co.za/bree\n\nFyn P 19\n\n5th floor, Speakers Corner, 37 Parliament Street, 021 286 2736 Peter Tempelhoff, Ashley Moss and Jennifer Hugé are the visionaries behind this brand new fine dining offering. A selection of set menus is available, including a business lunch menu, a Pescatarian menu and even a plant based menu. Among the intriguing ingredients on offer are idombolo, kombu biltong, enrigi mushrooms, ishikawa rice, wood fungus and Karoo truffle. Fynrestaurant.com\n\nHemelhuijs P 3\n\n71 Waterkant Street, 021 418 2042 Expect something different here where dishes contain tastes of the exotic. Try the salt-and-Sichuan-pepper calamari or a delicious cocktail or custom-blended coffee. hemelhuijs.co.za\n\nLa Tête P 4\n\n17 Bree Street, 021 418 1299 Giles and James Edwards have brought the unusual to this modern space, showcasing Giles’s 10-year experience as a chef in\n\nThe CCID’s Communications team promotes the Central City as a “live, work, play, stay and invest” destination that is inclusive and offers opportunities for all to enjoy. It does this through its brochures, publications, online platforms, and awareness campaigns it runs on behalf of the CCID’s other departments. Visit the CCID online at Capetownccid. org, Facebook.com/ CapeTownCCID, Twitter. com/CapeTownCCID and Instagram.com/ CapeTownCCID.\n\nTelling the Central City story\n\n##### 38 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020 CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\nLondon eateries. Salted hake, sweetbreads and ox heart make it worth repeat visits. latete.co.za\n\nMRKT Restaurant P 14\n\n57 Heerengracht Street, 021 111 0452 Located in the Onyx Apartment Hotel building, the restaurant is open to the public as well as hotel guests and serves wholesome breakfasts and lunches as well as a har vest-inspired buffet. There is a barista station where a variety of coffees is offered and a bakery producing handcrafted breads and pastries. There are on-the-go meals plus an enticing deli. newmarkhotels.com/places/in house/mrkt/\n\nRiverine Rabbit P 2\n\n81 Church Street, 021 424 7204 \nWith experience acquired at Cape Town’s \nfamous La Colombe and The Test Kitchen, \nchef Ash Heeger has teamed up with \nFrankie Fenner Meat Merchants and Publik \nWine Bar. The menu is small in size but \nhuge on flavour. Expect creative but always \ndelicious dishes. \nriverinerabbit.com \n\nThe Shortmarket Club P 2\n\n88 Shortmarket Street, 021 447 2874 Further evidence of the pulling power of the\n\nCBD is The Shortmarket Club, which forms part of award-winning chef Luke Dale Roberts’s culinary empire. Thanks to a dramatic interior and an imaginative menu, with the signature Dale-Roberts touch, this truly is a special-occasion venue. theshortmarketclub.co.za\n\nVilla 47 P 2\n\n47 Bree Street, 021 418 2740 \nThree eateries over three floors, all under \none roof at Villa 47. Locanda is the relaxed \nItalian bistro, where guests can taste regional \nspeciality dishes; upstairs at Stuzzico, you can \nenjoy a variety of Mediterranean-Asian tapas; \nand, topping it off, there’s a new fine-dining \nvenue, Pierino Penati. \nvilla47.co.za \n\nGRILLS\n\nAfro's Chicken Shop P 14\n\n31 Heerengracht Street, 087 365 3663 What started off as a food truck in KwaZulu Natal, has branched out, quite literally, to include locations in Cape Town, Durban and Johannesburg. As the name suggests, there is a whole lot of chicken on the menu. Afro's Chicken Shop serves traditional breakfasts, and for lunch you can choose from flame-grilled chicken portions or strips\n\nSticky BBQ\n\nP 5\n\n196 Long Street, 021 422 2361 These “pur veyors of ribs, wings, burgers, shakes and a good time”, also feature steaks, chops, hot wings, waffles and sides including regular fries, chicken cheese fries, cheese fries, chilli poppers and onion rings. Stickybbq.com\n\nSticky BBQ\n\neat Visit capetownccid.org\n\nCLOSEST PARKING (SEE PAGE 63)\n\nWALLET FRIENDLY\n\nA TREAT\n\nSPECIAL OCCASION\n\nCHILD FRIENDLY\n\nWHEELCHAIR FRIENDLY P\n\nCapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID with chips, of course, as well as burgers, wraps and chicken salads. All the décor is by local artists and artisans. Afroschicken.co.za\n\nBarcelos P 5\n\n216 Long Street, 021 424 8823 The Barcelos franchise brings its Portuguese flavour to the party revellers of Long Street, serving flame-grilled chicken in quarter-, half- and whole portions with various sauces, from the much anticipated Prego to the daunting-sounding Veri Peri. barcelos.co.za\n\nCarne SA P 5\n\n70 Keerom Street, 021 424 3460 As the name suggests, Carne restaurant is dedicated to the nation's most revered protein – meat – in this instance served with Italian flair. This carnivore’s paradise serves the finest cuts of Romagnola beef, Dorper lamb and game, which are presented to you beforehand so you can make your choice. One of the many highlights on the menu is the 1.2kg La Fiorentina T-bone steak, followed by chef Giorgio Nava’s legendary dark chocolate fondant following the consumption of which you will, most likely, not be able to move. carne-sa.com\n\nHQ P 2\n\n100 Shortmarket Street, \n021 424 6373 \nHeritage Square’s Headquarters is a Parisian \nstyle steakhouse that serves just one dinner \nmain: a sirloin steak with thin-cut fries and \nCafé de Paris butter. Simple, well-prepared, \nand delicious. \nhqrestaurant.co.za \n\nThe Famous Butcher’s Grill P 1\n\n101 Buitengracht, 021 422 0030 This restaurant in the Cape Town Lodge Hotel forms part of a franchise and specialises\n\nin superior A-grade steaks that have been matured to perfection. It’s a genuine meat lover’s paradise. capetownlodge.co.za\n\nHEALTH\n\nCity Bowl Health Kitchen P 25\n\n9 Commercial Street, 021 461 0334 This eatery, which is part Mexican, part vegetarian, specialises in a delicious array of healthy salads and juices. Pick your favourite ingredients from the build-your-own-salad selection. facebook.com/citybowlhealthkitchen\n\nThe Electric P 26\n\n48 Canterbury Street, 021 461 0916 If you're a fan of nut milks, be sure to check out this industrial-looking spot. You’ll find refreshing juices and smoothies as well as a “build-your-own egg-on-toast breakfast” at this eatery in a former auto electric workshop. In the owners’ own words, things may not always be quite what they seem. the-electric.com\n\nHokey Poké P 10\n\n1 Church Street, 021 422 4382 This was the first restaurant in Cape Town to specialise in the Hawaiian-style dish – raw fish served with veg and flavoured with soy sauce. Here, you can build your own or choose from the options available. Sit down and enjoy at the cute and cosy tables, or get it to go. hokeypoke.co.za\n\nHomage 1862 P 5\n\n168 Loop Street, 021 422 0900 Step into the conservatory and pay homage to the flame, in this stylish yet homely restaurant that pays homage to a host of seasonal meats, fish and delectable vegetable\n\nFood Inn - pg 41\n\nAfro's Chicken Shop - pg 38\n\nHomage 1862\n\nMRKT Restaurant - pg 38\n\n##### 40 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020 CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\ndishes that are all prepared to perfection on \nthe wood-fired grill. \nhomage.co.za \n\nLekker Vegan P 25\n\n37 Barrack Street \nVegans don’t have to miss out on the \nsatisfying smack of junk food. Get your vegan \nhamburgers, gatsbys (chip roll) and soft-serve \nice cream at this hip East City establishment. \nlekkervegan.co.za \n\nOrchard on Long P 5\n\n211 Long Street, 021 424 3781 Orchard serves a liver-cleansing array of juices, smoothies and health shots, designed to make you feel as good as you look, and was the first of its kind to open its doors in Cape Town. Locally grown fruit and vegetables are churned and blitzed into concoctions with enthusiastic names such as Beetroot Buzz and Exuberance. orchardonlong.co.za\n\nThe Poké Co P 2\n\n90 Loop Street, 087 470 0125 Vibrant, delicious and healthy, the poké bowls here will not disappoint. If fish is not your thing, there’s also chicken, and specials often include delicious broths. Choose from the menu or create your own. There’s even a poké burrito if you want to try something completely different. pokecoct.com\n\nPlant P 5\n\n8 Buiten Street, 072 521 9252 One of Cape Town’s first vegan restaurants, Plant still serves delicious smoothies, juices, salads, quesadillas, burgers and bowls. The focus is on natural, preservative-free ingredients, many of which are hand-produced. plantcafe.co.za\n\nQ What prompted you to open an Ethiopian restaurant in Cape Town?\n\nA At first it was just an art shop showcasing African art. Then it was suggested to me that I combine the concept with a restaurant, and so that’s what I did.\n\nQ Can customers buy the art and accessories on display in the restaurant?\n\nA They can but I do not want them to feel pressured to buy things. It is more important to me that customers get to experience what it is like to be invited into an Ethiopian home to share food.\n\nQ What do you love most about Cape Town?\n\nA The mountain and how close the ocean is on all sides.\n\nQ Where is the best coffee in Cape Town served?\n\nA At Madam Taitou, of course!\n\nQ What do you love the most about the neighbourhood in which your restaurant is located?\n\nA The city as a whole is filled with so many wonderful places that it is hard to choose. I love visiting the Castle, the Iziko South African Gallery and the Company’s Garden.\n\nQ Are most of your customers tourists?\n\nA No. They are a good mix of locals and tourists.\n\nPEOPLE OF\n\nTHE CITY\n\nSolomon Yigzaw, the owner of Madam Taitou in Long Street, has lived and worked in Cape Town for 20 years.\n\neat Visit capetownccid.org\n\nCLOSEST PARKING (SEE PAGE 63)\n\nWALLET FRIENDLY\n\nA TREAT\n\nSPECIAL OCCASION\n\nCHILD FRIENDLY\n\nWHEELCHAIR FRIENDLY P\n\nINDIAN\n\nBombay Brasserie P 10\n\nTaj Cape Town, cnr Wale Street and St Georges Mall, 021 819 2000 This opulent Indian restaurant in the Taj Cape Town uses only traditional ingredients and serves exquisite curries and seafood dishes. tajhotels.com\n\nEastern Food Bazaar P 19\n\n96 Longmarket Street, 021 461 2458, At this popular canteen-style eatery, a samoosa’s throw away from the Grand Parade, you will find Indian, Chinese, Malay and Turkish street food. Easternfoodbazaar.co.za\n\nFood Inn P 5\n\n156 Long Street, 021 422 5060 This is your one-stop-shop for a range of Indian dishes served in simple fast-food style (as well as gatsbys).\n\nKS Kitchen P 11\n\n22 Long Street, 021 418 1532 KS Kitchen serves a wide range of hearty dishes with an Indian flavour. From simple sandwiches to Cape Malay favourites and Indian curries that are bound to delight the senses. But don’t forget to try the Bunny Chows – there are four delectable options. Facebook.com/kskitchen\n\nMasala Dosa P 5\n\n167 Long Street, 021 424 6772 \nIt's little wonder this eatery earned an Award \nof Excellence from TripAdvisor. Delicious, \naffordable, healthy Indian food is served \nhere and the must-try dish is the masala \npotato curry with rice-and-lentil dosa crêpes. \nDosas are legendary, light-as-a-feather \nIndian-style crêpes. \nmasaladosa.co.za \n\nITALIAN\n\n95 Keerom P 5\n\n95 Keerom Street, 021 422 0765 \nThis elegant Milanese-influenced eatery is \nhoused in a 17th-century building, which \nwas originally the stables at The Company’s \nGarden. Expect dishes that are made from \nthe freshest ingredients, and that are both \nunpretentious and delicious. \n95keerom.com \n\nOrchard on Long - pg 40\n\nSTAY SAFE TIPS\n\nBocca P 1\n\nCnr Bree and Wale streets, 021 422 0188 Super-popular with Capetonians, Bocca's menu has been designed to incorporate a selection of dishes intended for sharing. The restaurant serves small plates, each of which is called Spoons, Fingers and Knives & Forks. The dishes are all jam-packed with flavour, but it’s the authentic Neapolitan pizza, cooked in a hand-built oven that was specially imported from Naples in Italy, that really entices diners. This is a modern, vibrant bar and restaurant where patrons are encouraged to linger longer. bocca.co.za\n\nCol’Cacchio P 4\n\nRedefine North Wharf, \n42 Hans Strijdom Avenue, \n021 419 4848 \nThis was the first branch of what has become \na very popular franchise to open in Cape Town \nfollowing on from a successful launch 26 \nyears ago in Johannesburg. It's a popular spot \nfor crisp, wood-fired pizzas. Grab a bunch of \nfriends, descend in a group and order from \nthe Half & Half range. There’s also a delicious \nbanting pizza on offer, as well as many \nspecials, so call beforehand if you're curious. \ncolcacchio.co.za \n\nThe Cousins Italian Trattoria P 25\n\n3b Barrack Street, 083 273 9604 The food is the hero at this established eatery, although the cousins themselves, who hail from Romagna on the Adriatic coast of Italy, will charm you too. It’s a delightful restaurant with quality cuisine – the pasta is made fresh every morning. thecousinsrestaurant.com\n\nNomNom on Long P 5\n\n166 Long Street, 021 424 7233 Looking for a fun and relaxed spot to grab an Italian-style pizza? NomNom (previously called the Clay Oven) does its own very special local take on the classic. It has its own open-fire pizza oven for baking and is a great choice for a relaxed dinner party in the winter months. It’s also open until 4am and,with its perfect position right in the middle of party-central Long Street, it’s great for those late-night, or shoud that be early morning, munchies. nomnomonlong.co.za\n\nTrue Italic P 4\n\n15 Bree Street, 021 418 7655 This tiny, award-winning “rustic café” takes pasta dishes to a new level. Italian sausages, imported cured meats and mozzarella are some of the stars of the show.
