A legendary Cape Town hangout, this popular tavern is great for a fun night out, for a quick drink and light meal before hitting the town, or for a get-together before taking in a show at the Fugard Theatre. diastavern.co.za.\n\nICE CREAM\n\nAngel Berry P 22\n\nFood Lover's Eatery, The Towers Building, cnr Heerengracht and Hertzog Boulevard, Foreshore This fabulous dessert parlour specialises in creamy ice cream, smooth frozen yoghurt, smoothies and pancakes. Delights on the menu are, believe it or not, low in sugar, halaal and simply delicious.\n\nMoro Gelato P 10\n\n165 Long Street, 021 422 3552 \nThe talented trio behind this 100% authentic \nItalian gelateria pride themselves on making \nfresh gelato every week using natural ingredients. \nTry the unforgettable pistachio, hazelnut, fragola \nand more. \nmorogelato.co.za \n\nCLOSEST PARKING (SEE PAGE 63)\n\nWALLET FRIENDLY\n\nA TREAT\n\nSPECIAL OCCASION\n\nCHILD FRIENDLY\n\nWHEELCHAIR FRIENDLY P\n\nmadam taitou 1 inside art gallery and madam taitou 2 77 long stret souvenir shop and many 76 b wale street tel: 021426 6969 more tel: 021 422 0949 glutten free vegan friendly best ambience one of the top ten resturants in cape town bookings is advisable\n\nThe Fugard Theatre Premier theatre & events complex in Cape Town.\n\nThe 320-seater Fugard Theatre and 120-seater Fugard Studio is located within the historic Sacks Futeran building in Cape Town’s District Six, with the renovated Congregational Church Hall in Caledon Street as its entrance. Construction of the Fugard Theatre was underwritten by its founding producer Eric Abraham, and is named in honour of Athol Fugard, South Africa’s greatest playwright. The Fugard Theatre is proud to present and host the very best of both local and international productions, bioscope and events.\n\nSEATING AND BOOKING All seating is reserved and can be booked through the theatre’s box office on 021 461 4554 or online at www.thefugard.com.\n\nPARKING \nHarrington Street car park is located at \nthe corner of Caledon and Harrington \nstreets and is available for the use of \ntheatre patrons. \nCaledon Street (Corner of Buitenkant), \nDistrict Six, Cape Town. \nwww.thefugard.com | 021 461 4554 \n\nthefugard.com @thefugard The Fugard Theatre @TheFugard\n\nFrom left: Sir Ian McKellen in Waiting for Godot | main entrance | main auditorium | Athol Fugard\n\nWest Side Story | Orpheus in Africa | King Kong | The Rocky Horror Show\n\nEATERIES IN 100 OUR GUIDE +\n\n### VENUES IN OUR GUIDE40\n\n+\n\nFOR MORE PLACES TO ENJOY, PLEASE VISIT WWW.CAPETOWNCCID.ORG\n\nBEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY play Visit capetownccid.org\n\nBARS & PUBS\n\nAces ‘n Spades P 11\n\n62 Hout Street, 021 424 1620 It’s delightfully decadent. The bar and dance floor are surrounded by dark timber, leather booths and gilt mirrors. thefirmct.co.za/aces-n-spades\n\nBeerhouse P 5\n\n223 Long Street, 021 424 3370 Beer aficionados love the spacious beer hall that spills out onto the street via a balcony and outside terrace. They are proud supporters of local craft beer as well as micro-brewers. On Friday and Saturday nights there is a DJ in the house and other events such as shebeen nights, karaoke nights and quiz nights are publicised on their website. Beerhouse.co.za\n\nBrewski \n168 Long Street, 021 426 4792 P 5 \nEnjoy great-tasting burgers and down them \nwith your favourite beers in this chilled \nestablishment situated right on the pulse \nof Cape Town’s Central City. \n\nCatacombs P 2\n\n107 Bree Street, 021 426 0330 Beneath La Parada, the Catacombs is an intimate lounge. Fresh products are used in the cocktails and Belvedere Vodka awaits, as do mirror balls and a pole. laparada.co.za\n\nCrowbar P 3\n\n43 Waterkant Street, 021 425 3420 You don’t get an older or more authentic pub experience than this one. More than 100 years old and unfailingly popular, cheerful and inexpensive, it remains cool. There’s a dance floor now too, so bring your boogie shoes.\n\nThe Dubliner at Kennedy’s P 5\n\n251 Long Street, 021 424 1510 This Irish pub brings Guinness-fuelled fun to Long Street, with a long bar, tiled floor and live music. Backpackers and locals alike pile in and continue the party at the club upstairs. dubliner.co.za\n\nDust and Dynamite P 27\n\n27 Caledon Street, 076 677 2947 Stepping into this rocking, rodeo-style saloon,\n\nCartel Rooftop Bar\n\nP 3\n\nInner City Ideas Cartel, 71 Waterkant Street, 076 336 2130 This rooftop bar is only open to the public for special events, so check out Cartel Rooftop on Facebook for details. The events often include the monthly gin o’clock and brunch, or just the opportunity to unwind over cocktails, burgers and tapas. cartelrooftop. co.za\n\nCartel Rooftop Bar\n\nWALLET FRIENDLY\n\nA TREAT\n\nSPECIAL OCCASION\n\nWHEELCHAIR FRIENDLY\n\nCLOSEST PARKING (SEE PAGE 63) P\n\nyou’ll feel like you’ve been dropped onto the set of a Spaghetti Western. With a rocking playlist to match the interiors, we recommend you swing open the saloon doors – not just for a beer but for an entire experience.\n\nEscobar Café/Bar P 26\n\n40-42 Canterbury Street, Zonnebloem, \n071 217 7009 \nGet a taste of Colombia at this gentleman’s \nbar with its friendly staff and Pablo Escobar \nthemed decor. \nescobar-cafebar.business.site \n\nGhibli Pool Bar P 3\n\nRadisson Blu Hotel, 22 Riebeeck Street, 021 467 4000 Enjoy chilling out at this sophisticated yet laid back cocktail bar when the sun sets. Lounge music, an array of cocktails, local wines, MCC and premium Champagne are on offer. radissonhotels.com/en-us/hotels/ radisson-blu-cape-town-residence\n\nThe Gin Bar P 10\n\n64 Wale Street, 071 241 2277 This bar from the team behind Honest Chocolate Café, with which it shares a courtyard, offers four artisan gin cocktails, called Head, Heart, Soul and Ambition. theginbar.co.za\n\nHank’s Olde Irish P 2\n\n110 Bree Street, 076 278 2376 \nA speciality whisky watering-hole, Hank’s is a \ncheerfully welcoming spot tucked behind Love \nThy Neigbour. Food is served too, as are wines \nand craft beers, but it’s the whisky that’s the \nreal draw card. \nhanks.co.za \n\nThe House of Machines P 2\n\n84 Shortmarket Street, 021 426 1400 Housed in an historic CBD building, THoM is a fusion of bar, café, bike workshop and menswear store. It’s an eclectic collaboration by high-profile industry figures, who say: “The philosophy behind this place is really simple: we’ve combined things we really dig…” thehouseofmachines.com\n\nLong Street Café P 5\n\n259 Long Street, 021 424 2464 This local favourite near the top of Long Street, identified by its neon sign and Art Deco windows, is popular for its great-value cocktails and bar food. It’s easy to while away a few hours here, people-watching at the outside tables.\n\nLove Thy Neighbour P 2\n\n110 Bree Street, 021 422 2770 Occupying the basement of a former church and spilling into a leafy courtyard, this popular watering hole offers a winning Germanic mix of craft beer, sausages and wine. Check its Facebook page for events. facebook.com/lovethyneighbourct\n\nThe Manila Bar P 5\n\n9 Green Road, 021 555 2064 The ideal spot for karaoke. With its chilled ambiance, the Manila Bar offers non-stop excitement in a friendly setting. facebook.com/themanilabar\n\nMarvel P 5\n\n236 Long Street, 021 426 5880 This laid-back bar pumps long after the sun has set. Grab a drink, hit the dancefloor or play some pool (there’s a table at the back).\n\nNo Reservations P 2\n\nHeritage Square, 92 Bree Street, 021 422 0128 Housed in a space beneath the Chefs Warehouse is this cosy bar, where the idea\n\nGET YOUR GROOVE ON\n\nARCADE: 152\n\nBree Street, arcadecafe.co.za\n\nRESET: 71 Loop Street, reset.co.za\n\nFICTION: 226 Long Street, facebook.com/ Fictiondjbar/\n\nDance the night away at these late-night hot spots.\n\nplay Visit capetownccid.org\n\n##### 48 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020 CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\nis that you sip on something while waiting for a (non-reservable) table upstairs. It’s small, intimate and perfect for drinks. chefswarehouse.co.za\n\nOrphanage P 5\n\nCnr Bree and Orphan streets, 021 424 2004 The drinks at this cocktail bar are every bit as remarkable as its styling. Gargoyles watch over artisan elixirs, twisted classics and tantalising tipples; gourmet snacks add to the fun. facebook.com/orphanageclub\n\nOpenwine P 1\n\n72 Wale Street, 021 422 0800 An Italian concept (known as the enoteca) has been cheerfully transplanted into Cape Town, occupying a cosy but smart space in Wale Street. Local wines are available for tasting (and buying) in the wine-concept store, which comprises three different venues, each offering a unique setting. Enjoy tasty bites along with your glass of wine in the Kitchen, relax in front of the TV in the Lounge or get the full experience (including a four-course meal) in the Cellar. openwineza.co.za\n\nPaparazzi Lounge P 5\n\nPepper Club, cnr Loop and Pepper streets, 021 812 8888 The Pepper Club hotel’s ground-floor bar, with its starry ceiling and gentlemen’s club chairs, is influenced by New York’s stylish after-hours haunts. Besides its classic Pepperclub burger and light meals, you can also enjoy high tea every afternoon. Keep an eye out for celebrity visitors who are often spotted here. pepperclub.co.za\n\nSaint Champagne Bar & Lounge P 26\n\n37 Harrington Street, 073 531 6312 Owner Toussaint Bashala sums up Saint as follows: “It’s a whole experience. We pride ourselves on our service, the DJs are on point and the bartenders are there to take care of our customers.” This is definitely the venue for a stylish night out, complete with star-studded parties, an extensive list of fine champagnes and an upmarket setting. saintlounge.co.za\n\nSky Bar and Daddy Cool P 11\n\nThe Grand Daddy, 38 Long Street, \n021 424 7247 \nThe Grand Daddy hotel has an unmissable \nbar located alongside the popular vintage \nAirstream caravans and outdoor cinema on its \nrooftop. Sipping a beer on the roof is a classic \nlocal experience that is not to be missed. \ngranddaddy.co.za \n\nSpacebar P 5\n\n197 Long Street, 083 325 0919 Styled as a dance club and nightclub, this drinks venue is open till late for the party set. You’ll find hip-hop, house, gqom and R&B on the decks and the cover charge is pretty reasonable. facebook.com/spacebaronlong\n\nSgt Pepper P 5\n\n194 Long Street, 021 424 5608 The combination of live music and pizza is what makes this establishment the perfect place to unwind after a hard day’s work. Pizza tastes better with good music, after all. sgtpepperct.com\n\nStones P 5\n\n166A Long Street, 021 424 0418 A Long Street institution, everyone is welcome at Stones. With a dance floor, a DJ booth, pool tables, VIP lounge and shooter bar, it’s the joint to be at. stones.co.za\n\nThe Slug & Lettuce P 5 218-224 Long Street, 021 424 7328 This small South African chain offers a jolly British experience. South African-flavoured\n\nWALK WITH confidence and don’t use your cellphone while on the move.\n\nDON’T WALK in deserted or dark areas.\n\nWATCH OUT \nfor sudden activity \nor a commotion; \nit may be a \ndistraction. \nKEEP ALL \npossessions close \nto your body and \nin sight. \n\nAVOID VISIBILITY of valuables, such as jewellery, cameras, laptops and tablets.\n\nDON’T CARRY large sums of money and avoid counting it in public.\n\nON THE STREET\n\nWALLET FRIENDLY\n\nA TREAT\n\nSPECIAL OCCASION\n\nWHEELCHAIR FRIENDLY\n\nCLOSEST PARKING (SEE PAGE 63) P\n\nshooters, snacks and mains are on offer. slugandlettuce.co.za\n\nTjing Tjing P 2\n\n165 Longmarket Street, 021 422 4920 Japanese food and culture are on offer in the 200-year-old heritage building. The menu offering includes gourmet burgers and a braai board. thevillageidiot.co.za\n\nThe Waiting Room P 5\n\n273 Long Street, 021 422 4536 Located above burger joint Royale Eatery, this venue hosts live music most nights. Bands play on Tuesdays, Wednesdays and Thursdays; hip-hop DJs hit the decks on Fridays; and house and disco rule on Saturdays. facebook.com/waitingroomct\n\nCLUBS\n\nClub 169 P 5\n\n227 Long Street, 021 422 3903 Get your groove on at this Cuban-inspired dance club and cigar bar that offers you a wide variety of cocktails and drinks to complement your evening. Step off Long and swing your hips to the R&B and hip-hop beats. club169.com\n\nCOCO P 2\n\n70 Loop Street, 072 239 5508 If you’re dressed to impress and looking for a menu to match, then pay a visit to this beautifully decorated club. Music ranges from hip-hop and house to a wide variety of other genres. cococpt.co.za\n\nCocoon P 16\n\n31st floor, ABSA Centre, 2 Riebeek Street, 021 421 0581 Take the lift all the way up to the 31st floor of one of Cape Town’s landmark ABSA towers and spend the night dancing, and enjoying the wraparound views of the CBD in a venue\n\nUncut\n\nP 5\n\n158 Long Street, 067 274 9794 This upmarket restaurant and bar offers you a dining experience by day and a dancefloor by night. Located in the hub of Cape Town’s Central City, you can start the night by en joying sundown ers and a burger special. Bookings for after 7pm are essential. Check the Facebook page for details of events, often featuring interna tional artists. facebook. com/uncutcpt\n\nUncut\n\nplay Visit capetownccid.org\n\n##### 50 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020 CapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\npacked to the rafters with the city’s beautiful \nand trendy set. \ncocoon.capetown.com \n\nFILM\n\nAlliance Française P 5\n\n155 Loop Street, 021 423 5699 Fans of international film are bound to find themselves in seventh heaven at the Alliance Française; the CBD’s “one-stop-shop for all things French since 1959”. This international cultural centre screens movies as part of its mission to introduce Cape Town to le monde francophone (the Francophone world). Free screenings take place most weeks, so it’s best to keep an eye on the website for all the details. The Alliance Française also has an impressive exhibition space and a media library. alliance.org.za\n\nPink Flamingo P 11\n\nThe Grand Daddy, 38 Long Street, 021 424 7247 This immensely popular outdoor cinema is perched on the roof of the Grand Daddy boutique hotel, alongside its famous rooftop trailer park of vintage Airstream caravans and a penthouse bar called SkyVar. It’s a fabulous location to sit back and enjoy the retro vibe as movies spanning all genres from the 1980s to 2000s play out in front of you. You get a free drink, bag of sweets and popcorn with your ticket or you can pre-order a fabulous picnic basket. Not to mention the view of the city spread out below you is pretty impressive. granddaddy.co.za\n\nTHEATRES\n\nAlexander Theatre P 11\n\n76 Strand Street, 021 300 1088 This is one of the liveliest little venues in the Cape Town CBD and is gathering a local following: upstairs, above the cosy bar and restaurant, you will find the tiny theatre that seats a maximum of 50 customers and\n\nQ What do you love most about the Cape Town city vibe; what makes it special?\n\nA It’s a fusion of the energy, the oddities, the diverse history that is ever-present in the landscape, and its people — from all walks of life, backgrounds and cultures.\n\nQ What is your favourite restaurant in the CBD?\n\nA This isn’t an easy question to answer as there are so many gems in the CBD. But if I had to choose, I’d go with SeaBreeze Fish & Shell. They offer superb sustainable seafood without breaking the bank.\n\nQ What is your favourite place to shop in the CBD?\n\nA The Earth Fair Food Market at St Georges Mall on Thursdays for all things foodie, and Clarke’s Bookshop for local literature.\n\nQ If you are spending a few days in the city, what do visitors need to experience while here?\n\nA Go anywhere with a rooftop, hike or take the cable car up Table Mountain. While the latter is a typical touristy thing to do, it offers unparalleled views of the city that are breathtakingly beautiful.\n\nPEOPLE OF\n\nTHE CITY\n\nAshraf Booley is a content marketing professional, poet and small business owner. In 2018 he was selected as a Mandela Washington Fellow, which gave him the opportunity to study Civic Leadership at Wagner College in New York. Ashraf is also the youngest chapter president of Impulse Group, a global social movement that focuses on gay men’s overall health.\n\nplays host to a range of innovative shows, cabaret experiences, musicians and one-act plays. Quiz nights take place on Wednesdays and the restaurant is open until midnight to accommodate those late-night hunger pangs. alexanderbar.co.za\n\nArtscape Theatre Centre P 24\n\nDF Malan Street, 021 410 9800 With an opera house, acclaimed theatre and arena space, Artscape, which was previously called the Nico Malan Theatre, is famous for hosting world-class international and local performances, including those by Cape Town City Ballet and Cape Town Opera (now an international, award-winning success). The theatre is home to the Jazzart modern dance company and is also the base for popular classical and jazz radio station Fine Music Radio. There is even a restaurant on site should theatregoers feel the need for a bite to eat. artscape.co.za\n\nGate 69 P 2\n\n87 Bree Street, 021 035 1627 Hosted by the glamorous, statuesque and inimitable Cathy Specific, this airline themed adult-focused cabaret-dining\n\nspot is always festive, packed with enthusiastic customers and filled with naughty laughter. Book a table in Economy class or opt for a spoil in First and prepare yourself for an entertaining and completely different experience. Make sure you dress for the occasion though, because casual wear is a no-no. gate69.co.za\n\nThe Cape Town City Hall P 18\n\nDarling Street, 021 444 0747 An institution with historical significance, the City Hall has witnessed more than a century of classical-music performances. It’s one of the CBD’s pre-eminent cultural venues and home of the Cape Philharmonic Orchestra. cpo.org.za\n\nThe Fugard Theatre P 26\n\nCnr Caledon and Buitenkant streets, 021 461 4554 Named after the legendary South African playwright Athol Fugard, this exciting venue stages new and well-loved musicals, plays and comedy sessions. There’s a strikingly modern \nbar and impressive restaurant. \nmarriott.com \n\nCape Heritage Hotel P 2\n\n90 Bree Street, 021 424 4646 This boutique hotel in the historic setting of Heritage Square is an urban oasis of teak ceilings and yellowwood floors. capeheritage.co.za\n\nThe Capetonian\n\nPier Place, 021 405 5670 The Capetonian has 170 rooms with views of Table Mountain or Table Bay and two restaurants, all close to the Cape Town International Convention Centre (CTICC). raya-hotels.com\n\nCape Town Hollow Boutique Hotel P 9\n\n88 Queen Victoria Street, 021 423 1260 Four-star facilities grace this inner-city haven, tucked away on a quiet street next to The\n\nCompany’s Garden. As an added bonus, \nkids stay for free. \nholidayinn.com/capetown \n\nHollow on the Square P 15\n\n9 Ryk Tulbagh Square, \n021 421 5140/021 423 1260 \nSituated on a leafy square, with restaurant \ntables spilling out onto the pavement, Hollow \noffers elegantly decorated hotel rooms. \nseasonsinafrica.com \n\nIcon Apartments P 4\n\nIcon Building, cnr Hans Strijdom \nAvenue and Lower Long Street, \n021 418 2821 \nFeaturing contemporary furnishings, these \napartments are swish urban pads. The rooftop \nswimming pool overlooks the CBD and there is \nsecure underground parking. \nhomefromhome.co.za \n\nLabotessa Hotel P 2\n\n5 Church Square, 37 Parliament Street, \n021 010 6600 \nClassic European style and luxury are on \nthe menu at this boutique hotel that offers \nunobstructed views of many of the city’s \nmost notable landmarks. If it gets stuck, \nfollow instructions \nprovided on \nthe ATM. \n\nMAKE SURE you are not followed after making a transaction.\n\n##### 56 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020 stay\n\nQ What makes the Cape Town CBD such a special place for you?\n\nA I love that it’s not a massive CBD, that within a few streets there’s a variety of experiences. I think for example a Bree street coffee shop and dining experience is quite different from the Long street scene, but both can be so much fun.\n\nQ What is your favourite restaurant in the CBD?\n\nA The Shortmarket Club for sure. It’s a bit pricey so not necessarily my regular spot, but it’s definitely a favourite.\n\nQ Do you have a favourite shop in the CBD?\n\nA To be honest, I’m not much of a shopper, as I generally only shop when I need something specific, and then I’ll go and buy only that item.\n\nQ Best coffee in the city?\n\nA Café Frank in Bree Street.\n\nQ Do you have a favourite night spot?\n\nA Blame it on age, but beyond restaurants, I haven’t been to a bar or night club in the city for years!\n\nQ If you are spending a few days in the city, what is the one thing you think visitors need to experience while there?\n\nA The Company’s Garden is always a great experience. Not only for the plants and the trees and general relaxation, but it’s also a fab spot to watch all sorts of Capetonians get on with their day. And, of course, the South African National Gallery is right there, which is a marvellous way to experience great South African art.\n\nPEOPLE OF\n\nTHE CITY\n\nAssociate Editor of the life and culture section of DailyMaverick.co.za, Malibongwe Tyilo may hail from the Eastern Cape, but Cape Town is his second home.\n\nParliament Hotel P 25\n\n9 Barrack Street, 021 461 6710 This establishment offers a range of services to make your stay as comfortable as possible. Even if you aren’t a hotel guest, you can enjoy breakfast here before going sightseeing. parliamenthotel.co.za\n\nPepper Club P 5\n\nCnr Loop and Pepper streets, \n021 812 8888 \nConnected to Long Street by a palm-lined \ncourtyard, this urban oasis offers fabulous Table \nMountain views from its rooftop pool. The opulent \ninteriors combine Capetonian cool with \ninternational sophistication. \npepperclub.co.za \n\nProtea Hotel Cape Town North Wharf P 4\n\n1 Lower Bree Street, 021 443 4600 This four-star establishment offers both apartment and hotel facilities. Amenities include a restaurant, rooftop pool, gym and secure parking. marriott.com\n\nRadisson Blu Hotel & \nResidence, Cape Town \nParking on site \n22 Riebeek Street, 021 467 4000 \nStylish architecture, an international menu \nprepared by seasoned chefs and magnificent \nviews of the harbour ensure that you’ll enjoy \nyour stay. \nradissonblu.com/en/hotel-capetown \n\nSignature Lux Hotel by ONOMO, Foreshore P 14\n\n31A Heerengracht Street, 087 688 0396 \nThere are lots of conveniences and amenities \non offer at this centrally situated hotel. There are \n157 rooms with queen-size extra-length beds, \nluxurious bathrooms, showers ony, as well as \nsmart TVs. Free Wi-Fi and a restaurant add to the appeal. 91loop.co.za\n\nCarnival Court Backpackers P 5\n\n255 Long Street, 021 423 9003 This is an unbeatable party spot located in the middle of Long Street’s bar district. It offers both dorms and private rooms – and earplugs if you find the buzz from the street a little intense. If you’re in Cape Town and looking to meet fellow travellers and hang out with like minded locals, the bar is where it’s at. carnivalcourt.co.za\n\nForty 8 Backpackers Hotel P 2\n\n48 Hout Street, 021 422 4848 Open 24 hours a day, 365 days a year, this backpackers hotel maintains it “caters for all adventurers”. Guests can look forward to free internet access, free tea and coffee, a breakfast room, free towel service and communal kitchen. Forty8backpackers.co.za\n\nLong Street Backpackers P 5\n\n209 Long Street, 021 423 0615 A mosaic stairwell leads you to one of Cape Town’s longest-serving backpacker hostels, consisting of converted apartments and great views over Long Street. longstreetbackpackers.co.za\n\nSun1 Foreshore\n\nParking on site \nMartin Hammerschlag Way, \n021 418 4664 \nEach of the 64 budget rooms features a \nshower, TV, working area, air-conditioning and \neco-lighting system. Breakfast and free Wi-Fi \ncomplete the package. \ntsogosun.com \n\nTwo Oceans Backpackers P 11\n\n47 Long Street, 021 422 5401 \nThis stylish backpacker lodge offers all the \nessentials: male and female dorms as well \nas double rooms; a kitchen, pool room and \nTV lounge. \ntwooceansbp.co.za \n\nUrban Hive P 5\n\n208 Long Street, 021 422 0565 Housed inside one of the CBD’s historic Victorian buildings, this hostel offers the perfect sanctuary to indulge in some rest and relaxation. Accommodation includes rustic dorms and private rooms. urbanhivebackpackers.com\n\nSTRAATWERK \nROAD \nMAINTENANCE \nTEAM \nThe CCID works with \nthis NGO to upskill \nindividuals who \nundertake road and \npavement repairs. \n\nessentials Visit capetownccid.org\n\nGETTING AROUND One of the great things about the Cape Town Central City is how easy it is to get around on foot. Within just under two square kilometres, you’ll find almost everything that you could want in a vibrant downtown – major museums, plenty of eateries, exciting art galleries, stores, bars and theatres.\n\nFor general info, call the City of Cape Town’s Transport \nInformation Centre (TIC), 24/7 on 0800 65 64 63. \nA great way to get about is by bicycle: Cape Town \nCycle Hire (021 434 1270, capetowncyclehire.co.za) and \nBike and Saddle (+27 76 540 4249, bikeandsaddle. \ncom). Or hire a motorbike or scooter at Cape Bike \nRentals (186 Bree Street, 021 426 5851). \nThe MyCiTi buses have revolutionised transport in \nCape Town. They’re clean, reliable and easy to access. \nNote: you can’t buy a ticket on board, it’s a no-cash \nsystem, so you do need to get a travel card in advance \nfrom major stations and selected convenience stores \n(myciti.org.za). The City Sightseeing Hop-On \nHop-Off buses are a great way to explore the city \n(citysightseeing.co.za). \nMinibus taxis are everywhere but they’re not \neveryone’s choice. There are numerous metered sedan \ntaxi companies. Rikkis (rikkis.co.za, 0861 745 547) \noffers shuttle, sharecab and door-to-door services; Excite \nTaxis (021 448 4444) is another option; and the popular \ninternational brand Uber (uber.com) is well established \nhere. \nIf you’d like to take a train on Metrorail (021 449 \n6478, metrorail.co.za) from the Cape Town Railway Station \nin Adderley Street, travel during peak times in first class \nand don’t take any valuables. \n\nINTERNET\n\nWi-Fi is widely available at hotels, and many cafés and restaurants offer free services to guests. The Company’s Garden offers a free service so you can check your Instagram feed, or pop in at the Cape Town Tourism Visitor Centre in Burg Street.\n\nHEALTH & WELLBEING\n\nCHEMISTS Barr’s Pharmacy 14 Long Street, 021 421 6756\n\nClicks Pharmacy – Christiaan Barnard \nCnr DF Malan Street and Rua Vasco Da Gama, \n021 441 000 \nClicks Pharmacy – Long Street \n2 Long St, 021 418 1003 \nClicks Pharmacy – Picbel Arcade \n58 Strand Street, 021 418 0567 \nClicks Pharmacy – St Georges Mall \n48 Adderley St, 021 418 3504 \nDis-Chem – Culemborg \nShop 1 St Georges Mall, The Heriot, 021 541 0049 \nLite-Kem Pharmacy \n24 Darling Street, 021 461 8040 \nOlsens Pharmacy \n129 Long Street, 021 423 2371 \nTothills Photo-Chemists \n18 Lower Burg Street, 021 421 2421 \nCLINICS & HOSPITALS \nDorp Street Reproductive Health Clinic \n3 Dorp Street, 021 483 4662 \nLasermed \n181 Bree Street, 021 424 5078 \nlasermed.co.za \nNetcare Christiaan Barnard Memorial Hospital \n25 DF Malan Street, 021 441 0000 \n· Accident and emergency \n021 441 0271 \nToll-free line: 0801 22 22 22 \n· Netcare Travel Clinic \nRoom 1107, 11th floor, Picbel Parkade, \n58 Strand Street, 021 419 3172, \ntravelclinic.co.za \n\nPEOPLE WITH A DISABILITY Major attractions and hotels in the Central City are disability friendly, but smaller establishments may not have wheelchair facilities. Wheelchair-friendly venues are listed in this guide.\n\nACCIDENTS & EMERGENCIES\n\nWe recommend that you take out adequate travel insurance \nbefore you leave home. If you require medical attention while \nhere, seek private treatment for smoother, quicker service. \nIn case of a medical or trauma emergency, call: \nER24 (private EMS) \n084 124 \nGeneral emergencies \n107 from a landline \n112 from a cellphone \nNational AIDS Helpline \n0800 012 322 \n\n##### 60 BEST OF CAPE TOWN CENTRAL CITY 2020\n\nessentials Visit capetownccid.org\n\nCapeTownCCID CapeTownCCID\n\nNetcare 911 (private EMS) \n082 911 \nPoisons Information Centre \n0861 555 777 \nConsumer Protector \n0800 007 081 \nWestern Cape Ambulance \n10177 \nMountain Emergencies \n021 937 0300 \nSea Rescue South Africa \n112 (from a cellphone) \n082 990 5962 (Bakoven) \n082 990 5963 (Table Bay) \n\nOPTOMETRISTS\n\nMullers Optometrists \n104 Longmarket Street, \n021 461 6254 \nSpecSavers \n52 St Georges Mall, \n021 424 4064 \nThe Saben Spectacle Co. \n22 Bree Street, \n021 418 1191 \n\nSECURITY\n\nThe CCID has a large contingent of uniformed public \nsafety officers who patrol the Central City. The 24-hour \nnumber is 082 415 7127. City-operated CCTV and \nmonitored parking marshals, together with these CCID \nofficers, have greatly improved security in the Cape Town \nCBD. Do use common sense, as you would in any big city: \ndon’t walk alone at night, don’t draw money in a quiet \narea, or expose cash, jewellery or cameras. \nPolice \n10111 \nCape Town Central Police Station \n021 467 8000/1/2/7 \nMetro Police \n021 480 7700 \n\nHARDWARE, LOCKSMITHS & SPECIALITY\n\nAAA Lazer Security \n16 Hans Strijdom Avenue, \n021 425 9966 \nC.D. Fox \n80 Hout Street, \n021 423 5206 \nCoastal Hire \n31C New Church Street, \n021 424 4900 \nHeddle Hardware \n76 Harrington Street, \n021 461 1206 \nLevers & Locks \n29 Loop Street, \n021 425 3841 \nMaster Keys \n9 Riebeek Street, \n021 419 6880 \n\nELECTRONICS\n\nCameraland \n68 Long Street, \n021 423 4150 \nCell C \nShop C87, Golden Acre Shopping Centre, Adderley Street, \n084 774 3003 \nMTN \n86 Shortmarket Street, \n083 869 1556 \nVodacom \nShop 22, Picbel Parkade, 58 Strand Street, \n021 421 0090 \nWeFix \n161 Loop Street, \n021 424 0900 \n\nLAUNDRY & REPAIR SERVICES\n\nBarksole (shoe repair) \n7 Riebeek Street, 021 421 7803 \nNannucci (laundry) \nShop 35 Golden Acre, Adderley Street, 021 421 7167 \n22 Bree Street, 021 421 4308 \n103 Parliament Towers, Plein Street, 021 465 5127 \n\nMONEY MATTERS\n\nThe Central City has approximately 120 ATMs. Major banks in South Africa are also here. However, we urge vis itors to be vigilant when using ATMs. Don’t let a stranger help you, protect your PIN and, if withdrawing cash, pack your money away while still at the ATM. Also, do not let anyone convince you that you need a “permit” to walk in the streets. You can report lost or stolen cards here: American Express: 0800 110929 Diners Club: 0860 346 377 MasterCard: 0800 990 418 Visa: 0800 990 475\n\nPOSTAL SERVICES\n\nThere are branches of the South African Post Office (postoffice.co.za) in the CBD: Grand Central 021 464 1701 and 154 Loop Street 021 424 7477. Postnet (0860 767 8638, postnet.co.za) is another, private option: 2 Long Street 021 418 1630, 2 Heerengracht Street 021 418 1717, 1 Mostert Street 021 461 1121, 6 Hout Street 021 426 0316 / 0839.\n\nSPORTS FACILITIES & GYMS\n\nBodytec \n42 Hans Strijdom Avenue, \n021 418 1523 \nIgnite Fitness \n122 St Georges Mall, \n021 422 1670 \nPlanet Fitness \nAtterbury House, cnr Lower \nBurg and Riebeek streets, \n087 135 1375 \n\nEMERGENCY NUMBERS\n\nAll emergencies including NSRI\n\n112 \n(From a cellphone) or \n107 \n(From a landline) \nNetcare 911 \n082 911 \nSouth African Police \n10111 \nor Cape Town Central \n021 467 8001/2/7 \nMetro Police \n021 480 7700 \nFire Department \n021 480 7700 \n24 Hour CCID \nControl \nCentre Number \n082 415 7127 \nPound for Pound Boxing Gym \n260 Bree Street, \n072 159 8361 \nSwitch \n186 Bree Street, 021 422 2515 \nVirgin Active Foreshore \n17 Rua Bartholemeu Dias Plain, \n021 418 5704 \nZone Fitness \n1st floor, 34 St Georges Mall, \n021 418 1255 \n\nTIPPING\n\nCape Town is a tipping city. For waiters, the usual tip is about 10-15%; for taxi drivers about 10%, and for porters about R10 a bag.\n\nTOURIST INFO\n\nFor specialist tourist advice, The Cape Town Tourism Visitor Centre is a one-stop-shop. It’s situated near Greenmarket Square, on the corner of Castle and Burg streets.
